L394: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L395: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Based on data from animal studies, vimseltinib can cause fetal harm. In Study No. DCC-3014-04-0011, after administration during the period of fetal organogenesis, structural abnormalities occurred at \ge 2.5 mg/kg/day (approximately 3 times the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). Fetuses from females receiving 2.5 and 5 mg/kg/day had increased incidences of small parietals and supernumerary suture bones. Fetuses from females receiving 5 mg/kg/day also had incomplete ossification of supraoccipitals. Fetuses from females receiving 15 mg/kg/day (approximately 23 times the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC) also had incomplete ossification of multiple sites including the supraoccipital, thoracic centra, and skull. Heart and liver malformations were also observed at 15 mg/kg/day as both organs were observed to be misshapen.
L396: In a fertility and early embryonic development study (Study No. DCC-3014-04-0020), male rats were administered 1, 2.5, or 5 mg/kg/day of vimseltinib starting 10 weeks before cohabitation, during cohabitation with untreated females, and at least 2 weeks post-cohabitation. Lower epididymal and testes weights were observed at 5 mg/kg/day (approximately 12 times the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). There were no treatment-related effects on mating, fertility, or sperm parameters at any dose tested. Female rats were administered 2.5, 5, or 10 mg/kg/day of vimseltinib 2 weeks prior to cohabitation with untreated males and during cohabitation until gestational day 7. Although there were no treatment-related effects on mating or estrous cycles, vimseltinib administered daily resulted in post-implantation loss at 10 mg/kg/day in female rats (approximately 20 times the exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC).
L397: The Applicant also performed a pre- and post-natal study (Study No. DCC-3014-04-0029) to observe the effects of oral administration of vimseltinib from the period of implantation to lactation. The reproductive health of the F1 generation was also assessed.
L398: F0 female rats were treated with 0.3, 1, or 3 mg/kg/day of vimseltinib starting on gestational day (GD) 6 until lactation day (LD) 20. A total of 6 F0 rats (1, 1, and 4 in the 0.3, 1, and 3 mg/kg/day groups respectively) were euthanized after total litter losses between LD 0-5. F1 rats from high dose females had reduced survival.

L402: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L403: ## 5.5.5.1. Phototoxicity
L404: The Applicant's Position:
L405: ## In Vitro 3T3 Neutral Red Uptake Phototoxicity Assessment of Vimseltinib
L406: An in vitro 3T3 neutral red uptake assay was conducted to assess potential phototoxicity of vimseltinib. The relative reduction in viability of BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts exposed to vimseltinib and ultraviolet radiation (+UVR; 5 J/cm2 of UVA and 18 or 19 or 20 mJ/cm2 of UVB), was compared with the viability of fibroblasts exposed to vimseltinib in the absence of ultraviolet radiation (-UVR). Relative cell viability remained unchanged across all vimseltinib concentrations under both non-irradiation and irradiation conditions: thus, IC50 values could not be calculated due to the lack of cytotoxicity. Vimseltinib was not phototoxic to BALB/c 3T3 cells in the presence or absence of simulated solar light.
L407: The FDA's Assessment:
L408: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position.
L409: ## 5.5.6. Safety Margins
L410: The Applicant's Position:
L411: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L412: The calculated safety margins for the definitive repeat dose toxicity studies with vimseltinib were calculated by dividing the total AUC0-24 from the respective toxicology study at the NOAEL, STD10, or HNSTD with the human total steady state AUC0-24 (13,400 ng*hr/mL) dosed at 30 mg twice weekly (Population Pharmacokinetic Modeling Report, eCTD 5.3.3.5). The margins were based on exposures that were available at the end of the dosing period unless otherwise noted. Margins were determined based on sex combined group mean values unless otherwise noted. Total exposures were used given that vimseltinib is highly protein bound in mouse, rat, dog and human plasma with fraction unbound of 2.8%, 0.65%, 3.3%, and 3.3%, respectively.

L428: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L429: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L430: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L431: The Clinical Pharmacology review focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosage, use in participants with hepatic impairment, and potential drug-drug interactions (DDI).
L432: The Applicant's proposed dosage of vimseltinib 30 mg orally twice weekly, with a minimum of 72 hours between doses, with or without food is generally acceptable based on the integrated analysis of PK along with the dose- and exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety.
L433: The current submission included data evaluating the effect of vimseltinib on the PK and safety in patients with mild hepatic impairment. As there was an insufficient number of patients with moderate hepatic impairment using the classification from the National Cancer Institute's Organ Dysfunction Working Group (NCI-ODWG), a post-marketing requirement (PMR) was issued to evaluate the PK and safety of vimseltinib in participants with moderate hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG).
L434: In addition, vimseltinib's potential for DDIs was inadequately addressed. Based on in vitro data, vimseltinib was shown to inhibit BCRP, OATP1B1/OATP1B3, and OCT2, but no clinical data is available to determine the clinical consequence of these DDIs. Therefore, three PMRs were issued to evaluate vimseltinib with substrates of BCRP, OATP1B1/OATP1B3, and OCT2.
L435: ## 6.1.1. Recommendations
L436: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in NDA 219304 and recommends approval. They key review issues and recommendations are summarized in Table 15.
L437: **Table 15:** FDA-Key Clinical Pharmacology Issues and Recommendations
L438: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness is the ORR observed in Study MOTION. See Section 8.1.
General dosing instructions	The Applicant's proposed dosage of 30 mg orally twice weekly, with a minimum of 72 hours between doses, with or without food is supported by the ORR observed in 83 patients in the vimseltinib arm and safety data from 253 patients receiving vimseltinib from Study MOTION and other clinical trials.
L439: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	
Hepatic Impairment: No dosage adjustment is recommended in patients with mild hepatic impairment (bilirubin \le ULN and AST > ULN or bilirubin > 1 to 1.5 x ULN and any AST). The recommended dosage in patients with moderate-or-severe hepatic impairment has not been established (bilirubin > 1.5 to 3 x ULN and any AST; bilirubin > 3 x ULN and any AST, respectively).
Renal Impairment: No dosage adjustment is recommended in patients with mild-or-moderate renal impairment (eGFR \ge 30 mL/min). The recommended dosage in patients with severe renal impairment has not been established (eGFR < 30 mL/min).
Age: No clinically significant differences in vimseltinib's PK were observed based on age (20 to 91 years).
Sex and Race: No clinically significant differences in vimseltinib's PK were observed based on race (Asian, Black or African American, White).

Drug-drug interactions	
Avoid concomitant use of vimseltinib with P-gp substrates. If concomitant use of a P-gp substrate is unavoidable, administer vimseltinib at least 4 hours before taking the P-gp substrate unless otherwise recommended in the substrate Prescribing Information.

QTc Assessment	
At the maximum recommended dose of vimseltinib, clinically significant QTc interval prolongation was not observed. However, as vimseltinib causes concentration-dependent increase in the QTcF interval and the impact of hepatic impairment on vimseltinib exposure has not been characterized, the effect on the QTcF interval in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment is unknown.

Bioavailability between the to-be-marketed and clinical trial formulations	
Study MOTION did not administer the commercial formulation of vimseltinib, and no clinical study compared the commercial formulation to the formulation used in Study MOTION. Instead, the Applicant is seeking a biowaiver.

Outstanding issues	A PMR was issued to evaluate vimseltinib's effects in patients with moderate hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG).
Vimseltinib inhibits substrates of BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 in vitro and has the potential to cause drug interactions with substrates of these transporters. A
L440: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
clinical drug-interaction study with substrates of these transporters will be conducted as PMRs.
Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable.
L441: Source: FDA generated table.
L442: ## 6.1.2. Post-Marketing Requirements and Commitments
L443: The rationale and descriptions of PMRs and PMC are summarized in Table 16.
L444: **Table 16:** FDA-Post-Marketing Requirements Pertinent to Clinical Pharmacology
L445: PMR or PMC	Rationale	Description
PMR	The risk of hepatic impairment on increasing vimseltinib plasma exposure, leading to increased adverse reactions, has not been ruled out and additional data is needed to determine the appropriate dosage of vimseltinib in patients with moderate hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG).	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial of vimseltinib in patients with moderate hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG).
PMR	Vimseltinib inhibits BCRP in vitro. The potential inhibition of vimseltinib may increase the systemic concentrations of BCRP substrates, which may increase the potential toxicity of BCRP substrate drugs.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial of vimseltinib with substrates of BCRP.
PMR	Vimseltinib inhibits OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 in vitro. The potential inhibition of vimseltinib may increase the systemic concentrations of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 substrates, which may increase the potential toxicity of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 substrate drugs.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial of vimseltinib with substrates of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3.
PMR	Vimseltinib inhibits OCT2 in vitro. The potential inhibition of vimseltinib may increase the systemic concentrations of OCT2 substrates, which may increase the potential toxicity of OCT2 substrate drugs.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial of vimseltinib with substrates of OCT2.
L446: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L447: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L448: ## The Applicant's Position:
L449: The clinical PK profile of vimseltinib was investigated in 6 clinical studies.
L450: Three Phase 1 clinical pharmacology studies in healthy participants have been completed:
L451: - A PK study including evaluation of food effects (DCC-3014-01-002)
- A human absorption, metabolism, and excretion study (DCC-3014-01-003)
- A study to assess the potential for drug-drug interactions with a P-gp inhibitor and a gastric acid-reducing agent (DCC-3014-01-006)
L452: One Phase 1 clinical pharmacology study is ongoing:
L453: - A hepatic impairment study (DCC-3014-01-004)
L454: Two patient studies are ongoing:
L455: - The supportive Phase 1/2 first-in-human study (DCC-3014-01-001) in participants with TGCT and advanced malignant solid tumor (MST)
- The pivotal Phase 3 study DCC-3014-03-001 (MOTION) in participants with TGCT
L456: Population PK (PopPK) analysis of data from healthy participants and participants with TGCT and advanced MST was conducted to characterize the PK of vimseltinib and the effects of intrinsic and extrinsic factors on the PK of vimseltinib. Physiological-based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling and simulation were performed to assess drug-drug interaction liability of vimseltinib as an inducer of CYP2B6, inhibitor of permeability glycoprotein (P-gp), breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP), and organic cation transporter 2 (OCT2). Exposure-response (E-R) analyses were conducted to investigate relationships between vimseltinib exposure and efficacy and safety endpoints and evaluate the suitability of the investigated dosing regimen of 30 mg twice weekly.
L457: Concentration QTc modeling was performed using data from participants with advanced MST from the Phase 1/2 study (DCC-3014-01-001) and data from healthy participants from DCC-3014-01-002.
L458: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L459: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position, except for the following:
L460: - Physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling and simulation was also used to evaluate vimseltinib as an inhibitor of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3.
L461: - The Applicant intends to perform exploratory PK analyses based on NCI classification for moderate and severe hepatic impairment cohorts after the NDA's PDUFA action date.
L462: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L463: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L464: ## The Applicant's Position:
L465: The totality of evidence shows that vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly is the optimal dosing regimen in patients with TGCT. The efficacy, safety, and PK/PD data from the Phase 1/2 study (DCC-3014-01-001) were evaluated to determine the recommended dose to evaluate in the Expansion Cohorts of the Phase 1/2 Study and the Phase 3 MOTION study. The 30 mg twice weekly dose studied in the dose escalation cohort of the Phase 1/2 study was selected based on the manageable safety profile, PK, pharmacodynamic, and the objective responses observed.
L466: A loading dose was not recommended as the simulated median time to steady state in participants with TGCT receiving the 30 mg twice weekly dose of vimseltinib was similar with or without 5 daily 30 mg vimseltinib loading doses.
L467: The available clinical data from the pivotal MOTION study, along with efficacy and safety data collected to date from the Phase 1/2 study, continue to support vimseltinib administration at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly with no loading dose in participants with TGCT.
L468: In the pivotal MOTION study, vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly demonstrated statistically significant improvements in ORR for participants with TGCT at Week 25 compared to placebo based on independent radiological review (IRR) per Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST) v1.1 (40% versus 0%; p&lt;0.0001) and per tumor volume score (TVS) (67% versus 0%; p&lt;0.0001). In addition, the study met all key secondary endpoints demonstrating statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvements in range of motion (ROM), physical function, worst stiffness, QoL, and worst pain compared to placebo. Adverse events observed with vimseltinib were manageable.
L469: Maximal vimseltinib target engagement in participants with TGCT was demonstrated by dose-dependent reduction in nonclassical monocytes (NCMs) and a steep increase in CSF1 levels at 30 mg twice weekly and 10 mg daily doses of vimseltinib.
L470: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L471: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. The proposed dosage of vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly is supported by the available data; however, limited dose ranging and dose optimization was performed. See FDA's assessment on Section 6.3.2.2 for more details.
L472: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L473: ## The Applicant's Position:
L474: ## Intrinsic Factors
L475: ## Hepatic Impairment
L476: Hepatic excretion is expected to account for a substantial portion of the elimination of vimseltinib. The potential impact of baseline hepatic function on vimseltinib exposure and safety is currently being investigated in Study DCC-3014-01-004. The interim PK analysis of the results from participants with mild hepatic impairment (as per Child-Pugh classification) and their matched (by weight, sex, race and age) controls with normal hepatic function demonstrated that following a single 10 mg vimseltinib dose:
L477: Mild hepatic impairment resulted in 42% lower geometric mean C_{max} for total vimseltinib compared to matched participants with normal hepatic function (geometric mean AUC_{0-inf} was 24% lower, but the 90% CIs included 100%). There was no evidence of increased total vimseltinib exposure in participants with mild hepatic impairment.
L478: There was no evidence of increased total DP-7005 exposure in participants with mild hepatic impairment.
L479: Mild hepatic impairment did not affect protein binding of vimseltinib nor DP-7005.
L480: There was no evidence of concentration-dependent protein binding.
L481: Liver function based on National Cancer Institute classification was not identified as a covariate in the PopPK analysis.
L482: No dose adjustment is recommended in patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A).
L483: The safety and efficacy of vimseltinib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B and C) have not been established; evaluation of the PK of vimseltinib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B and C) is ongoing.
L484: ## Renal Impairment
L485: Renal excretion is not expected to account for a substantial portion of the elimination of unchanged vimseltinib. Based on the PopPK analysis, where estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) was not identified as a covariate, no relevant differences in steady state exposure are anticipated for mild and moderate renal function (eGFR \ge 37.7 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2).
L486: No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. No clinical data is available in patients with severe renal impairment; a recommended dose of vimseltinib has not been established in patients with severe renal impairment.
L487: ## Race
L488: In the PopPK analysis, an exploratory covariate-parameter relationship was identified for participants of Black or African American race, who had a ~1.3-fold higher apparent peripheral volume of distribution (V_p/F) compared to non-Black or African American participants. However, the effect on steady state exposure parameters was within the 80% to 125% limits.
L489: ## Participants With TGCT Versus Healthy Participants
L490: In the PopPK analysis, healthy participants had a ~0.7-fold lower apparent central volume of distribution (V_c/F) and a ~2.1-fold increased V_p/F compared to participants with TGCT. Healthy participants were estimated to have a 1.13-fold higher maximum concentration during a dosing interval at steady state (C_{max,ss}) than that of the reference participant. The effect did not exceed the 80% to 125% reference area.
L491: ## Other Intrinsic Factors
L492: In the PopPK analysis, the effect of body weight was included using allometric scaling with fixed exponents such that participants with higher body weight had higher apparent clearance (CL/F), V_c/F, V_p/F, and apparent inter-compartmental clearance (Q/F). Participants with lower body weight had a higher vimseltinib exposure in terms of average concentration during a dosing interval at steady state (C_{avg,ss}), C_{max,ss}, and minimum concentration during a dosing interval at steady state (C_{min,ss}) compared to heavier participants. The effects of the other identified covariates were limited and did not exceed the 80% to 125% reference area. Therefore, no dose adjustments based on body weight is recommended.
L493: ## Extrinsic Factors
L494: ## Effects of Other Drugs on Vimseltinib
L495: ## P-gp Inhibitors
L496: Concomitant administration of single-dose vimseltinib with 200 mg of itraconazole (a P-gp inhibitor) once daily showed that whilst peak exposure (C_{max}) to vimseltinib was comparable when administered alone and in the presence of itraconazole, total exposure to vimseltinib (area under the plasma concentration curve for a specified time [AUC_{0-t}] and area under the plasma concentration curve from zero to infinity [AUC_{0-inf}]) was approximately 17% to 22% higher in the presence of itraconazole, suggesting that P-gp inhibitors have a weak effect on vimseltinib.
L497: The coadministration of a P-gp inhibitor reduces vimseltinib CL/F by 18%. Since the change in steady-state exposure is driven by the change in CL/F for drugs with a long half-life such as vimseltinib, this reduction in CL/F was used in the E-R analyses to predict the proportions of safety events following administration of vimseltinib administered with and without a P-gp inhibitor. Following 30 mg twice weekly dosing, the proportions of safety events at Week 25 were predicted to be similar when vimseltinib was administered with and without a P-gp inhibitor.
L498: ## Proton Pump Inhibitors
L499: Concomitant administration of vimseltinib with rabeprazole (a proton pump inhibitor) 20 mg once daily reduced peak (C_{max}) and total exposures to vimseltinib (AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf}) by approximately 21% to 26% suggesting that proton pump inhibitors have a weak effect on vimseltinib absorption.
L500: ## Effects of Vimseltinib on Other Drugs
L501: ## CYP Isoform-selective Substrates
L502: Vimseltinib caused (b) (4). Based on PBPK modeling, no interaction was predicted following coadministration of bupropion, a CYP2B6 substrate, with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly.
L503: ## Drug Transporter Systems
L504: Vimseltinib inhibited P-gp, BCRP and OCT2 in vitro in clinically relevant concentrations.
L505: ## P-glycoprotein Substrates
L506: Based on PBPK modeling, (b) (4). However, weak to moderate interaction was predicted following coadministration of dabigatran with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly. Simulations showed that the risk of dabigatran interaction with vimseltinib can be eliminated when dabigatran etexilate is administered (b) (4) hours after vimseltinib.
L507: ## BCRP Substrates
L508: Based on PBPK modeling, (b) (4).
L509: ## OCT2 substrates
L510: Based on PBPK modeling, (b) (4).
L511: For food effect, refer to Section.
L512: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L513: FDA generally agrees, except for the following:
L514: ## Hepatic Impairment
L515: - FDA considers hepatic impairment using the NCI-ODWG criteria more clinically relevant than the Child-Pugh classification.
- Study DCC-3014-01-004 has evaluated participants with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh). This study had an insufficient number of patients with mild hepatic impairment
L516: (NCI-ODWG), and no patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (either Child-Pugh or NCI-ODWG).
L517: - Based on the PopPK analysis of hepatic impairment using NCI-ODWG, mild hepatic impairment did not significantly affect the PK of vimseltinib. However, this analysis was limited to participants with mild hepatic impairment. There were no participants with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG) in the popPK analyses.
L518: ## Renal Impairment
L519: - FDA agrees that the PopPK analysis suggests no impact of renal impairment on vimseltinib clearance for patient with eGFR \ge 37.7 mL/min/1.73 m^2. Additionally, the human mass balance study provides supportive evidence that renal impairment is unlikely to affect clearance because renal excretion played a limited role in vimseltinib's elimination. See Section 6.3.1 for more details.
L520: ## DDI - Effects of Vimseltinib on Other Drugs
L521: - FDA does not classify interactions with transporters using weak or moderate categories.
- Vimseltinib inhibited OAT1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, and MATE2-K in vitro at clinically relevant concentrations, in addition to P-gp, BCRP, and OCT2.
- FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position on the DDI risk with P-gp substrates. Vimseltinib was predicted to increase dabigatran's AUC and C_{\max} up to 3-fold when considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC_{50} value of P-gp. The inhibitory effects of vimseltinib may be mitigated by administering dabigatran 4 hours after vimseltinib dosing.
L522: For details, see Section 19.4.3. See Section 6.3.2.4 for FDA's recommendation on P-gp substrates.
L523: - FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position on the DDI risk with BCRP substrates. Vimseltinib was predicted to increase rosuvastatin's AUC and C_{\max} up to 2.6- and 6.3-fold, respectively, when considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC_{50} value of BCRP.
- The Applicant's model with rosuvastatin evaluated vimseltinib's effects on a substrate of BCRP, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3. For details, see Section 19.4.3.
- FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position on the DDI risk with OCT2 substrates.
L524: For details, see Section 19.4.3.
L525: ## Food Effect
L526: - See Section 6.3.1 for details on FDA's assessment.
L527: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L528: ## The Applicant's Position:
L529: As discussed during the Pre-NDA meeting, the Phase 1 study DCC-3014-01-004 evaluating the effects of hepatic impairment on the PK of vimseltinib is ongoing. An interim PK analysis of the data from participants with mild hepatic impairment was submitted in the NDA.
L530: The PK results from the moderate and severe hepatic impairment cohorts will be submitted to the NDA upon completion.
L531: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L532: FDA generally agrees, except for the following:
L533: - Study DCC-3014-01-004 has evaluated participants with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh). This study had an insufficient number of participants with mild hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG), and no participants with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (either Child-Pugh or NCI-ODWG).
- PopPK analysis of hepatic impairment using NCI-ODWG was limited to participants with mild hepatic impairment. There were no participants with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG) in the popPK analyses.
L534: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L535: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L536: ## Data:
L537: The nonclinical pharmacology and PK profile of vimseltinib was investigated by in vitro studies with human biomaterials, including assessments of plasma protein binding, metabolism, the potential for induction or inhibition of CYP enzymes, interactions with transporters, and hERG channel current inhibition.
L538: In human plasma, protein binding of vimseltinib at 10 µM was demonstrated to be 96.5%; thus, the free fraction of vimseltinib was determined to be 3.5%. Among the animal species tested, the percent bound values at the same concentration ranged from 95.4% in monkey to 99.3% in rat. The bound percentage of DP-7005 (the *N*-des-*iso*-propyl active metabolite of vimseltinib) in human plasma was 92.4%. The percent bound in animals ranged from 84.7% in monkey to 98.3% in rat.
L539: The half-life of vimseltinib in human hepatocyte preparations was \ge 4 hours, as no substrate loss was observed after 240 minutes of incubation. In metabolite identification studies in human hepatocytes, DP-7005 was the only ultraviolet-detectable metabolite. ^{14}\text{C}-vimseltinib was metabolized in human hepatocytes and microsomes via oxidation, *N*-dealkylation, and dehydrogenation. No Phase 2 conjugated metabolites were detected. Metabolites detected in
L540: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L541: human hepatocytes and microsomes were also found in other species. In addition to unchanged vimseltinib, up to 7 metabolites were tentatively identified/characterized in hepatocytes and microsomes. In human hepatocytes, &gt;96% of the radioactivity was associated with unchanged ^{14}\text{C}-vimseltinib. In human microsomes, &gt;90% of the radioactivity was associated with unchanged ^{14}\text{C}-vimseltinib. DP-7005 accounted for &lt;3% radioactivity in human microsomes. Reaction phenotyping studies in microsomal preparations and recombinant human CYP enzyme incubations to characterize human CYP enzyme activity indicated a minor role of CYP enzymes in the human phase 1 metabolism of vimseltinib and minimal risk for drug-drug interactions involving vimseltinib CYP phase 1 metabolism.
L542: In vitro, both vimseltinib and DP-7005 exhibited \text{IC}_{50} values &gt;40 \mu\text{M} for all CYP isoforms studied, and \text{IC}_{50} values &gt;100 \mu\text{M} were exhibited by vimseltinib for 5 out of 7 CYP isoforms. Additionally, neither vimseltinib nor DP-7005 exhibited time-dependent or metabolism-dependent inhibition of any CYP isoform, demonstrating little to no risk for time- or metabolism-dependent inhibition of CYP isoforms. Vimseltinib and DP-7005 exhibit a low/minimal liability for a drug-drug interaction mediated by CYP inhibition.
L543: Treatment of cultured human hepatocytes with up to 100 \mu\text{M} vimseltinib demonstrated low to minimal risk for CYP3A4 or CYP1A2 enzyme induction and a slight risk for CYP2B6 induction at anticipated clinical exposures, suggesting a potential risk for perpetrator drug-drug interactions involving CYP metabolism. CYP2B6 induction was further investigated in the PBPK modeling, which did not predict any interaction following coadministration of bupropion (a CYP2B6 substrate) with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly.
L544: Nonclinical studies indicated that vimseltinib may alter the PK of concurrent medications that are substrates for BCRP, P-gp, and OCT2.
L545: Potential effects of vimseltinib on cardiac electrophysiology were tested using a GLP hERG patch clamp electrophysiology assay. At concentrations of 10 \mu\text{M} and 30 \mu\text{M}, vimseltinib did not significantly inhibit hERG channel current. In addition, vimseltinib (0.1 \mu\text{M} to 25 \mu\text{M}) exhibited negligible binding to the hERG channel in a non-GLP in vitro binding assay.
L546: ## Clinical Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetics
L547: ## Pharmacokinetics
L548: The clinical pharmacology of vimseltinib was studied in the ongoing Phase 1/2 study in participants with TGCT and advanced MST, in the ongoing MOTION study in participants with TGCT, and in 4 Phase 1 clinical pharmacology studies (one of which is ongoing).
L549: The Phase 1/2 study, MOTION, and Study DCC-3014-01-002 contributed data for the PopPK analysis and the E-R analysis. Physiologically based pharmacokinetic modeling and simulations were performed using data from Study DCC-3014-01-002, Study DCC-3014-01-003, Study DCC-30140-01-004 (Part 1 only), and Study DCC-3014-01-006.
L550: ## Absorption, Distribution, Metabolism, and Excretion
L551: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L552: ## *Absorption*
L553: Vimseltinib was rapidly absorbed across the dose range studied in the clinical pharmacology studies, with single doses that ranged from 6 mg to 30 mg and repeat doses that ranged from 40 mg to 50 mg once daily.
L554: After oral administration of a single 30 mg dose of vimseltinib (given as 3 capsules each containing 10 mg) under fasted conditions in Study DCC-3014-01-002, median time to maximum drug concentration (T_{max}) ranged from 1 to 2 hours. C_{max} ranged from 305 to 433 ng/mL, and AUC_{0-inf} ranged from 42,900 to 59,100 h\cdotng/mL.
L555: In the Phase 1/2 study, after oral administration of a 30 mg dose of vimseltinib on Cycle 1 Day 1 to participants with TGCT in dose escalation, median T_{max} and mean C_{max} were consistent with the results from Study DCC-3014-01-002: median T_{max} ranged from 1.60 to 1.70 hours, and C_{max} ranged from 273 to 320 ng/mL.
L556: Due to its pH dependent solubility, vimseltinib has the potential to be subject to a drug interaction with acid-reducing agents. Study DCC-3014-01-006 evaluated the drug interaction potential of a single dose of vimseltinib as a victim drug when co-administered with the proton pump inhibitor rabeprazole in healthy participants. The results suggested concomitant administration of proton pump inhibitors has a weak effect on vimseltinib absorption.
L557: Administering vimseltinib with a high-fat meal decreased the rate of vimseltinib absorption, possibly due to delayed gastric emptying, but there was no food effect on the extent of absorption.
L558: ## *Distribution*
L559: Mild hepatic impairment did not appear to affect vimseltinib plasma protein binding as geometric mean unbound vimseltinib fraction remained between approximately 6% and 7% in participants regardless of hepatic function classification or concentration.
L560: The PopPK model suggests an apparent volume of the central compartment of 88.0 L and an apparent volume of the peripheral compartment of 28.9 L. Covariate analysis suggested that healthy participants had a lower V_c/F, and higher V_p/F, compared to participants with TGCT. However, the effect on steady-state exposure parameters was within the 80% to 125% limits.
L561: Individual whole blood/plasma ratios of total radioactivity in Study DCC-3014-01-003 ranged from 0.559 (at 10.0 hours) to 0.898 (at 0.5 hours). Based on AUC_{0-inf}, the geometric mean whole blood-to-plasma total radioactivity ratio was 0.751, indicating no appreciable red blood cell partitioning.
L562: ## *Metabolism*
L563: Metabolic profiling as part of Study DCC-3014-01-003 determined that primary metabolism occurred by oxidation, *N*-demethylation, and *N*-dealkylation; secondary biotransformation pathways included *N*-demethylation, dehydrogenation, and oxidation. In male healthy participants, 15 detectable metabolites were produced, 8 of which were identified. No major
L564: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L565: circulating metabolites were detected. The most abundant, albeit minor metabolite was M3 (DP-7005), accounting for 9.67% of the dose in urine and 5.63% of the dose in feces. DP-7005, like vimseltinib, is a CSF1R inhibitor (IC_{50} value of 65 nM compared to 2.8 nM for vimseltinib).
L566: In Study DCC-3014-01-003, peak and overall exposure to the minor active DP-7005 metabolite in plasma was appreciably lower than that observed for vimseltinib. Geometric mean molecular weight-corrected metabolite-to-parent ratios (MPRs) for C_{max}, AUC_{0-t}, and AUC_{0-inf} were 2.58%, 4.48%, and 5.01%, respectively. The MPRs in Study DCC-3014-01-002, Study DCC-3014-01-004, and Study DCC-3014-01-006 were in a similar range.
L567: ## *Elimination*
L568: Elimination of vimseltinib occurred by metabolism and urinary and fecal excretions. In Study DCC-3014-01-003, when extrapolated to infinity, 37.5% of the administered ^{14}C-vimseltinib dose was excreted in urine and 42.9% in feces.
L569: Following single-dose administration of 30 mg vimseltinib to healthy participants in a fasted condition in Study DCC-3014-01-002, geometric mean (geometric coefficient of variation) CL/F ranged from 0.507 L/h to 0.699 L/h. Terminal elimination phase half-life (t_{1/2}) ranged from 134 to 137 hours (39.6%). Following single-dose administration of 30 mg vimseltinib to healthy participants in Study DCC-3014-01-006, CL/F and t_{1/2} were in a similar range.
L570: In the PopPK analysis, the median effective half-life of vimseltinib based on all participants in the analysis population was 136 hours, consistent with the t_{1/2} observed in Study DCC-3014-01-002.
L571: ## **Dose Proportionality**
L572: In Study DCC-3014-01-002, dose proportionality was confirmed in healthy participants for vimseltinib C_{max} and AUC_{0-24h} following single oral doses of vimseltinib 6 mg to 50 mg since the slopes (\beta_1) in the linear regression for these parameters were approximately 1 and the 90% confidence intervals (CIs) of the slope estimates included the value of 1 (C_{max}: 0.9572 [0.8464, 1.0680]; AUC_{0-24h}: 0.9343 [0.8376, 1.0309]). Additionally, vimseltinib AUC_{0-inf} was dose proportional between 6 mg and 30 mg; the linear regression slope (90% CIs) was 0.8804 (0.6802, 1.0805).
L573: In the Dose Escalation Phase of the Phase 1/2 study, plasma exposure of vimseltinib following single-dose administration of vimseltinib 20 mg to 30 mg, as measured by geometric mean plasma AUC_{0-4h}, AUC_{0-8h}, and C_{max}, increased with dose in participants with TGCT. Following multiple doses, the trend for higher exposure associated with higher dose was maintained in participants with TGCT receiving daily maintenance doses. Increased exposure for DP-7005 was associated with higher doses.
L574: ## **Steady State**
L575: In the PopPK analysis, simulations based on empirical Bayes estimates of the full analysis population showed that the 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen of vimseltinib results in a
L576: median accumulation ratio of 2.59. The simulated median time to steady state in participants with TGCT receiving the 30 mg twice weekly dose was similar (28 days) with or without 5 once daily 30 mg loading doses.
L577: ## Effect of Food
L578: Vimseltinib can be taken with or without food.
L579: Administering vimseltinib with a high-fat meal decreased the rate of vimseltinib absorption, possibly due to delayed gastric emptying, but there was no food effect on the extent of absorption.
L580: Following administration of 30 mg vimseltinib with a high-fat meal, C_{max} of vimseltinib was approximately 30% lower compared to fasted conditions (geometric LS means ratio [90% CIs] of 69.86% [62.92%, 77.58%]), and median T_{max} was delayed by 5 hours (6.0 hours compared to 1 hour under fasted conditions).
L581: AUC_{0-120h}, AUC_{0-t}, and AUC_{0-inf} for vimseltinib were comparable under fed and fasted conditions with the ratios of geometric LS means and the corresponding 90% CIs within the accepted 80.00% to 125.00% limits.
L582: The PopPK analysis suggested that fed participants had an approximately 6-fold longer mean absorption time (MAT) than fasted participants; however, this had a limited effect on steady state exposure metrics.
L583: ## Effect on QT Interval
L584: A concentration QTc modeling analysis was performed based on the data from participants with MST in the Phase 1/2 study and healthy participants from Study DCC-3014-01-002 (who are physiologically similar to participants with TGCT). The results from the cardiodynamic evaluation of the 40 mg and 50 mg doses demonstrated that vimseltinib has no clinically relevant effect on heart rate or on cardiac conduction (the PR and QRS intervals). There was no clinically meaningful effect on the QTc interval, and an effect on \Delta\Delta QTcF exceeding 10 ms can be excluded within the observed ranges of plasma concentrations of vimseltinib, up to 1774 ng/mL.
L585: ## Exposure-Response Analyses
L586: Data from the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION were used to inform the E-R model development and analysis.
L587: The exposure-response relationships between vimseltinib C_{avg,ss} and ORR per RECIST v1.1 and per TVS at Week 25 in participants with TGCT from MOTION was well characterized by a logistic regression model. An exposure-efficacy analysis based on data from participants with TGCT did not show association between vimseltinib exposure and ORR per RECIST v1.1 or TVS. A flat exposure-efficacy relationship was identified across the exposure range for the evaluated dosing regimen.
L588: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L589: The exposure-safety relationships between vimseltinib C_{max,ss} and the time to first event of periorbital edema, rash, AST elevation, and alanine aminotransferase (ALT) elevation in patients with TGCT or MST from the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION were well characterized by Cox proportional hazards models. For all safety endpoints, the event incidence was estimated to increase with increasing C_{max,ss}, with an initial increase for low C_{max,ss}, followed by a plateau. The impact of vimseltinib exposure was more pronounced for AST elevation events, while the relationship was relatively flat for the other endpoints. For periorbital edema, rash, and ALT elevation, the difference in the median predicted proportions of these safety events at Week 25 was marginal at &lt;5% following 20 mg twice weekly, compared to 30 mg twice weekly. For AST elevation, the difference was -7.3%.
L590: ## Pharmacodynamics
L591: The pharmacodynamic effect of vimseltinib on NCMs (circulating monocytes in peripheral blood, which express CSF1R and are sensitive to anti-CSF1/CSF1R targeted therapies), and plasma CSF1 levels were investigated as biomarkers to study vimseltinib target engagement.
L592: Changes in pharmacodynamics biomarkers were assessed among the participants with TGCT and MST from the safety population in the Phase 1/2 study. Specifically, the percentage change from baseline in NCM and fold changes in the levels of CSF1 in plasma were summarized by visits across Dose Escalation cohorts up to Week 25.
L593: Vimseltinib demonstrated a dose-dependent decrease in the levels of circulating NCM in Dose Escalation cohorts who received 10 mg to 40 mg twice weekly doses and 6 mg to 20 mg daily doses. The mean reduction in NCM levels from baseline at Cycle 2 Day 1 in participants with TGCT was in the range of 65% to 80%. The maximum NCM reduction was reached by Cycle 2 Day 1 (Week 5) for most cohorts and was maintained throughout the dosing period.
L594: Increased levels of CSF1 in plasma were observed across all cohorts. A steep increase in mean fold changes over baseline CSF1 levels at Cycle 2 Day 1 was observed from 20 mg twice weekly dose to 30 mg twice weekly, or from 6 mg daily dose to 10 mg daily dose. The fold changes in CSF1 levels in participants with TGCT were in a similar range, except for the lower dose cohort (6 mg daily dose). Similar to the NCM changes, the maximum fold changes in CSF1 levels across the cohorts were reached by Cycle 2 Day 1.
L595: ## The Applicant's Position:
L596: The PK and PD characteristics of vimseltinib, together with the E-R analyses, support the proposed administration of vimseltinib at 30 mg twice weekly in patients with TGCT.
L597: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L598: FDA generally agrees, except for the following:
L599: ## Food Effect
L600: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L601: Although there was no significant impact of food on AUC_{0-inf} and AUC_{0-t}, C_{max} was decreased in the presence of food (Table 17). This decreased C_{max} is not considered clinically meaningful, since only a small decrease in exposure was seen, and changes in C_{max} are not expected to significantly impact antitumor activity.

L606: ## Absorption
L607: - FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of absorption. A biowaiver was requested to bridge the formulation between the commercial formulation and the formulation used in study MOTION. The biowaiver was found to be acceptable. For details, see the review of ROMVIMZA by the Office of Product Quality.
L608: ## Metabolism
L609: - DP-7005 is not considered an active metabolite, as DP-7005 is unlikely to play a role in efficacy or safety. DP-7005's activity is 23-fold lower than the parent drug, and systemic levels of DP-7005 in the body appear too low to achieve clinical activity (i.e., DP-7005's IC_{50} is lower than its C_{max}). Furthermore, DP-7005's impact on safety was not evaluated, as DP-7005 was a minor metabolite in humans.
- Vimseltinib (b) (4)
- Vimseltinib is a P-gp substrate but is not a substrate of BCRP, BSEP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2-K.
- Vimseltinib inhibited OAT1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, and MATE2-K in vitro at clinically relevant concentrations, in addition to P-gp, BCRP, and OCT2.
L610: ## Excretion
L611: - Following a single oral dose of radiolabeled vimseltinib, approximately 43% of the dose was recovered in feces (9.1% unchanged) and 38% in urine (5.1% unchanged) after a single radiolabeled dose. Plasma metabolites were minor (&lt;10% of total plasma radioactivity exposure).

L628: exposure is not yet known, it is uncertain what the impact on QTc interval might be in patients with moderate or severe liver impairment.
L629: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L630: ## 6.3.2.1. Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L631: ## The Applicant's Position:
L632: Yes. Clinical studies evaluating vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly in participants with TGCT have demonstrated strong clinical benefit and manageable safety profile, with PK/PD and E-R data supporting the recommended dose (see Section 6.2.1 and 6.2.2 of this Assessment Aid).
L633: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L634: FDA agrees that the completed clinical studies provide supportive evidence of effectiveness of vimseltinib in the proposed patient population. See Section 6.2.
L635: ## 6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L636: ## The Applicant's Position:
L637: Yes. The recommended dosing regimen of vimseltinib in patients aged 18 years and older is 30 mg twice weekly at least 3 days apart with or without food. If a vimseltinib dose is missed by more than 2 days, the patient should skip the missed dose and return to the regular dosing schedule. This regimen is supported by its pharmacological properties and by clinical efficacy and safety data generated to date.
L638: Vimseltinib dose may be temporarily withheld if the recommended dose is not tolerated. Vimseltinib may be resumed on a reduced dose as shown in the table below when the clinical condition improves. Vimseltinib should be discontinued in patients unable to tolerate a 14 mg dose.
L639: ## **Recommended Dose Reduction for Adverse Reactions**
L640: Dose Reduction	Twice Weekly Dose
First	20 mg
Second	14 mg
L641: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L642: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L643: FDA generally agrees that the proposed dosage is appropriate for the general patient population based on the dose- and exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety. Doses should be separated by a minimum of 72 hours, as directed on the blister package. The blisterpack is anticipated to reduce patient nonadherence to the dosing regimen.
L644: ## Dose-Response and Exposure-Response Relationships
L645: In Study DCC-3014-01-001, three dose levels were evaluated in patients with TGCT with the following efficacy results (Table 19).

L649: At the data cutoff (DCO) of September 5, 2020, which was when the Applicant proposed the Recommended Phase 2 Dose (RP2D) to FDA, the Applicant chose Cohort 5 based on (1) Cohort 5 having 1 CR, whereas Cohort 8 had none, and (2) Cohort 5 having a lower or similar incidence of the most common TEAEs, compared to Cohort 8, in the Applicant's opinion.
L650: In MOTION, 39% of patients experienced a dose reduction and 48% of patients remained on 30 mg by Week 25. The following data support the dosage of 30 mg twice weekly:
L651: - There appeared to be a positive relationship for dose and efficacy in the Phase 1/2 study for ORR. However, the exposure-efficacy analysis was inconclusive and was performed with limited doses (20 mg twice weekly (BIW) and 30 mg BIW), and for the single dose, the trend appeared to be flat.
- The DOR was similar between responders who had dose reductions to those who did not.
- In the exposure-safety analysis, which was conducted in 285 participants, including 12 participants with 6 mg QD exhibited the following trends:Shallow, positive trends for periorbital edema, rash, and ALT increase.
- No apparent trend for CPK increase.
- A steep, positive trend for AST increase. However, AST increases led to dose reductions/interruptions in only 2.5% of patients treated with vimseltinib in MOTION in the double-blind and open-label period.
L652: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L653: - There was a significant overlap in C_{max,ss} between patients who experienced asthenia, blood CPK elevation, periorbital edema, pruritus, and pooled rash and rash maculopapular events, compared to patients who didn't experience those events.
- A more convenient dosing (i.e., a once daily dosing of 10 mg) would not improve the safety profile or dosage modifications, since it would only have a minor decrease in C_{max,ss}, based on limited data from the Phase 1/2, dose-finding study which had approximately 12 patients per cohort.
L654: For details on the E-R analyses, see Section 19.4.2 for the pharmacometrics team's assessment.
L655: Based on the PK profile, FDA recommends the following instructions when the patients misses the correct dose timing or if vomiting occurs.
L656: ## Missed Dose
L657: As vimseltinib has a relatively long half-life (t_{1/2} = 134 to 137 hours), missing a dose by 48 hours wasn't anticipated to significantly impact the steady state concentrations. This was evaluated further in the following popPK simulations (Table 20).
L658: **Table 20:** FDA-Summary Statistics of Simulated Vimseltinib PK Parameters for Medication Error Scenarios on Week 6 (W6)
L659: PK Parameters (Week 6)	30 mg BIW for 6 weeks (Reference)	30 mg BIW for 5 weeks followed by 30 mg on D1 and D2 on Week 6	30 mg BIW for 5 weeks followed by 30 mg on D1 and D3 on Week 6
AUC0-168h,W6 (h*mg/L)	
Geomean (gCV)	95.5 (48.7)	102 (48.2)	98.7 (48.4)
Median (min, max)	95.9 (21.9, 381)	102 (23.5, 396)	99.1 (22.8, 389)
C_{max, W6} (ng/mL)	
Geomean (gCV)	828 (44.8)	950 (42.6)	885 (43.7)
Median (min, max)	838 (217, 2960)	957 (270, 3120)	893 (241, 3040)
L660: Abbreviations: Geomean=Geometric mean. gCV=geometric coefficient of variation. N=1810 for each simulation.
Source: Applicant's analysis.
L661: Based on Table 20, if doses were administered 24 hours apart, such increase in C_{max} does not appear to be clinically meaningful (i.e., 14%). Therefore, missing the dose by 48 hours is also not anticipated to significantly impact C_{max}. A missed dose of &gt;48 hours was not considered, since the minimum dosing interval was 72 hours apart. Taking two doses within 24 hours would increase the C_{max} by 2-fold, based on PK linearity, and would be expected to worsen the safety. Therefore, FDA recommends the following for missed doses: If a dose is missed by 48 hours or less, take the missed dose as soon as possible and take the next dose on its regularly scheduled day. If a dose is missed by more than 48 hours, skip the missed dose, and take the next dose on its regularly scheduled day.
L662: ## Vomiting
L663: FDA recommends the following for vomiting: If vomiting occurs within 30 minutes of taking a dose, repeat that dose. Otherwise, take the next dose on its regularly scheduled day.
L664: Tmax was evaluated to determine recommendations for vomiting. As the Tmax ranged between 0.5 to 4 hours, 30 minutes was selected, as this was on the lower range of Tmax, which would avoid potentially duplicating dose administration and increasing toxicity.
L665: ## **6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?**
L666: ## The Applicant's Position:
L667: No. Based on the assessment of intrinsic factors, no dose adjustment or change in regimen is required based on demographics or in special populations. A summary of the effects of intrinsic factors on PK is presented in Section .
L668: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L669: FDA generally agrees, with the clarifications and exceptions noted in Section 6.2.2.2.
L670: ## **6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?**
L671: ## The Applicant's Position:
L672: ## **Food Effect**
L673: Administering vimseltinib with a high-fat meal decreased the rate of vimseltinib absorption, possibly due to delayed gastric emptying, but there was no food effect on the extent of absorption.
L674: Following administration of 30 mg vimseltinib with a high-fat meal, Cmax of vimseltinib was approximately 30% lower compared with fasted conditions (geometric LS Means ratio [90% CIs] of 69.86% [62.92%, 77.58%]) and median Tmax was delayed by 5 hours (6.0 hours compared with 1.0 hour under fasted conditions).
L675: AUC0-120h, AUC0-t, and AUC0-inf for vimseltinib were comparable under fed and fasted conditions with the ratios of geometric LS means and the corresponding 90% CIs within the accepted 80% to 125% limits.
L676: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L677: The PopPK analysis suggested that fed participants had an approximately 6-fold longer MAT than fasted participants; however, this had a limited effect on steady-state exposure metrics. In conclusion, vimseltinib can be taken with or without food.
L678: ## Effect of Other Drugs on Vimseltinib
L679: ## *CYP Inhibitors and Inducers*
L680: Vimseltinib is metabolically stable in human microsomes and recombinant CYP enzymes, indicating a minor role of CYP enzymes in the human phase 1 metabolism of vimseltinib and minimal risk for victim drug-drug-interactions involving CYP phase 1 metabolism.
L681: ## *Inhibitors of Transporters*
L682: Nonclinical data indicated that vimseltinib is a weak substrate of P-gp and exhibits low potential to be a victim of a drug-drug interaction mediated by P-gp inhibitors. Study DCC-3014-01-006 evaluated the drug interaction potential of a single dose of vimseltinib as a victim drug when co-administered with a strong P-gp inhibitor, itraconazole, in healthy participants.
L683: Concomitant administration of itraconazole 200 mg once daily showed that while peak exposure (C_{max}) to vimseltinib was comparable, total exposure (AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf}) was approximately 17% to 22% higher, suggesting that P-gp inhibitors have a weak effect on vimseltinib.
L684: The co-administration of a P-gp inhibitor reduces vimseltinib CL/F by 18%. Since the change in steady-state exposure is driven by the change in CL/F for drugs with a long half-life, such as vimseltinib, this reduction in CL/F was used in the E-R analyses to predict the proportions of safety events following administration of vimseltinib administered with and without a P-gp inhibitor. Following 30 mg twice-weekly dosing, the proportions of safety events at Week 25 were predicted to be similar when vimseltinib was administered with and without a P-gp inhibitor.
L685: ## *Gastric Acid-reducing Agents*
L686: Due to its pH-dependent solubility, vimseltinib has the potential to be subject to a drug interaction with acid-reducing agents. Study DCC-3014-01-006 evaluated the drug interaction potential of a single dose of vimseltinib as a victim drug when co-administered with the proton pump inhibitor rabeprazole in healthy participants.
L687: Concomitant administration of rabeprazole 20 mg once daily reduced peak (C_{max}) and total exposures (AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf}) to vimseltinib by approximately 21% to 26%, suggesting that proton pump inhibitors have a weak effect on vimseltinib absorption.
L688: ## Effect of Vimseltinib on Other Drugs
L689: ## *CYP Induction*
L690: Vimseltinib demonstrated low to minimal risk for CYP3A4 or CYP1A2 enzyme induction during in vitro studies with human hepatocytes. A slight risk was demonstrated for CYP2B6
L691: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L692: induction at anticipated clinical exposures, suggesting a potential risk for perpetrator drug-drug interactions involving CYP metabolism.
L693: PBPK modeling to simulate the extent of drug-drug interaction between vimseltinib and bupropion (a CYP2B6 substrate) did not predict any interaction following co-administration of bupropion with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly.
L694: ## *Inhibition of Transporters*
L695: Nonclinical studies indicated that vimseltinib may alter the PK of concurrent medications that are substrates for BCRP, P-gp, and OCT2.
L696: PBPK modeling was performed to simulate the extent of drug-drug interaction between vimseltinib and [REDACTED](b) (4) and dabigatran (P-gp substrates), rosuvastatin (a BCRP and OATP1B1/3 substrate), and [REDACTED](b) (4) (an OCT2 substrate).
L697: Weak to moderate interaction was predicted following co-administration of dabigatran or [REDACTED](b) (4) with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly.
L698: Simulations showed that the risk of dabigatran interaction with vimseltinib can be eliminated when dabigatran etexilate is administered [REDACTED](b) (4) hours after vimseltinib.
L699: P-gp and [REDACTED](b) (4) substrates should be taken at least [REDACTED](b) (4) and [REDACTED](b) (4) hours after vimseltinib administration, respectively, to reduce the risk of potential drug interactions.
L700: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L701: Food Effect: FDA agrees that food does not have a clinically significant impact on vimseltinib PK and can be taken without regards to meals. See Section 6.3.1 for more details.
L702: DDI and PBPK: FDA is recommending a 4-hour separation between vimseltinib and P-gp substrates, based on PBPK risk assessment. FDA also disagrees with the Applicant's assessment of DDIs on CYP3A induction in vitro, inhibition of transporters, and the PBPK modeling on transporters. See Section 6.2.2.2 and Section 19.4.3 for more details.

L732: ## The Applicant's Position:
L733: Table 21 provides a listing of clinical studies relevant to this application.
L734: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L735: The MOTION study was the basis for efficacy and safety (Section 6 of the USPI for vimseltinib) approval of vimseltinib for the treatment of adult patients with symptomatic TGCT for which surgical resection will potentially cause worsening functional limitation or severe morbidity was based on the results from Week 25 of Part 1 (double-blind portion). The review was based on the 123 patients who were randomized to receive vimseltinib (n=83) or placebo (n=40). One patient in the placebo arm was not eligible for efficacy evaluation as the patient discontinued treatment within one month after providing consent. All patients received at least one dose of vimseltinib 30 mg.
L736: The pooled safety population (n=253) to support Sections 6 of product labeling reflects exposure to vimseltinib in 83 patients with TGCT enrolled in the double-blind portion and in 35 patients in the open-label portion who crossed over to vimseltinib in MOTION, and in 135 patients with TGCT or solid tumors in other clinical trials. This safety pool contributed to the evaluation of the incidence of any adverse event and any serious adverse events that may have occurred at a low incidence.
L737: As part of the review strategy, FDA conducted an independent review of efficacy data for MOTION. The results from the supportive trial, DCC-3014-01-001, were reviewed for safety; however, the data for efficacy were not independently verified by the review team. For the purposes of safety evaluation regarding hepatotoxicity, FDA evaluated n=253 patients were treated with at least one dose of vimseltinib. Patients enrolled in the pharmacologic studies DCC-3014-01-002, 01-003, 01-004, and 01-006 which enrolled healthy volunteers who received a single-dose of vimseltinib were not included in the pooled safety population (n=253)
L738: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L739: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L740: ## 8.1.1. DCC-3014-03-001 (MOTION)
L741: ## Trial Design
L742: ## The Applicant's Description:
L743: This is a pivotal Phase 3, placebo-controlled study of vimseltinib in patients with TGCT, consisting of 2 parts: Part 1 was double blind and Part 2 was open label.
L744: The double-blind and randomized design of this study allowed for adequate assessment of vimseltinib compared to a control group. The selection of placebo as the control group was justified because in most participating countries there were no approved systemic, nonsurgical therapies for the treatment of TGCT in patients not amenable to surgery. While pexidartinib is approved in the United States, it is associated with a risk of potentially fatal liver injury and available under restricted access (REMS program) (TURALIO, USPI 2023).
L745: The study design is outlined in Figure 8. The study consisted of a 42-day screening period prior to the first dose of study drug, a Part 1 double-blind treatment period of 24 weeks (referred to in 28-day cycles) and a Part 2 open label period until Week 49.
L746: Participants were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to receive either vimseltinib at the dose of 30 mg twice weekly or placebo for 24 weeks. Interactive response technology was used to randomize and assign study drug. Randomization was stratified for tumor location (lower limb/all other) and region (U.S./non-U.S.).
L747: After completion of the End of Part 1 assessments, participants who wished to continue to the open-label part of this study (Part 2) were unblinded. Participants randomized to placebo crossed over to receive vimseltinib and participants initially randomized to vimseltinib continued to receive vimseltinib in Part 2.
L748: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L749: **Figure 8** Motion Study Schema
L750: ## **Patient population:**
L751: - Histologically confirmed, symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumors
- Surgical resection will potentially cause worsening functional limitations or severe morbidity
- No prior use of systemic therapy targeting CSF1 or CSF1R (except imatinib and nilotinib)
L752: Abbreviations: biw=twice weekly; CSF1=colony-stimulating factor 1; CSF1R=colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor; MRI=magnetic resonance imaging; n=number of participants in a category; N=sample size; PRO=patient-reported outcome; ROM=range of motion.
L753: a Disease follow-up was performed for up to 2 years.
L754: Note: Participants were eligible to receive study drug until radiological confirmation of disease progression as defined in the protocol, unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal by participant, physician's decision, or commercial availability of vimseltinib and for as long as vimseltinib was being developed to support the indication, and continuation of treatment did not conflict with the Sponsor's right to terminate the study.
L755: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L756: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study design. The dose studied in MOTION was 30 mg orally taken twice weekly. Patients were allowed to undergo surgical resection only after completion of Part 1.
L757: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L758: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L759: Symptomatic patients \ge18 years of age with histologically confirmed TGCT for whom surgical resection could potentially cause worsening functional limitation or severe morbidity were eligible. Patients who previously received anti-CSF1/CSF1R therapy (except for imatinib or nilotinib) were excluded.
L760: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L761: The Applicant highlighted a few eligibility criteria which the review team agrees with; however, the review team included the following additional key eligibility criteria:
L762: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L763: - Patients must have symptomatic disease in a joint where ROM can be assessed.
- Patients must have symptomatic disease with at least mild or moderate pain or stiffness.
- Patients must have bilirubin, AST and ALT levels \le upper limit of normal (ULN).
- Patients must have disease that is likely to worsen, or result in significant morbidity, if surgery is performed. A surgical team review prior to enrollment was required for all patients to provide specific rationale for why surgery was likely to worsen morbidity.
- If a patient requires analgesic, analgesic use must be stable. Patients must complete 14 consecutive days of questionnaires during the screening period and be able to complete PRO via an electronic device.
- Patients may have been pretreated with systemic therapy such as imatinib or nilotinib and may not have been previously treated with systemic therapy that targets CSF1 or CSF1R.
- Patients with active liver or biliary disease including nonalcoholic steatohepatitis (NASH) or cirrhosis were excluded.
L764: ## Study Endpoints
L765: ## The Applicant's Description:
L766: ## Primary Endpoint
L767: - ORR (including complete response [CR] and partial response [PR]) per RECIST v1.1 at Week 25
L768: ## Key Secondary Endpoints
L769: - ORR per TVS at Week 25
- Change from baseline in active ROM of the affected joint, relative to a reference standard, at Week 25
- Change from baseline in the Patient-reported Outcomes Measurement Information System -Physical Function (PROMIS-PF) score at Week 25
- Change from baseline in the Worst Stiffness numeric rating scale (NRS) score at Week 25
- Change from baseline in euroQol visual analogue scale (EQ-VAS) (also known as 5-level EuroQual-5D [EQ-5D-5L] VAS) at Week 25
- Response of at least a 30% improvement in the mean Brief Pain Inventory (BPI) Worst Pain NRS score without a 30% or greater increase in narcotic analgesic use at Week 25
L770: ## Other Secondary Endpoints:
L771: - ORR per RECIST v1.1
- ORR assessed by mRECIST at Week 25
L772: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L773: - Duration of response (DOR) (time from first PR or CR to disease progression or death) assessed using RECIST v1.1, TVS, and mRECIST
- Incidence of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), treatment-emergent serious adverse events (SAEs), related TEAEs, dose reductions, dose interruptions, and discontinuation of study drug due to AE
- Changes from baseline in laboratory parameters, ECGs, and vital signs
L774: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L775: TGCT is a slow-growing progressive and debilitating but non-lethal disease. ORR may be used to measure the reduction in tumor burden over time. Due to the slow growing nature of the tumor and the symptomatic and functional impacts of the tumor location, supportive evidence of clinical benefit based on clinical outcome assessments (COAs) that can evaluate improvement in pain, range of motion, and physical function is useful to understand the drug effect.
L776: FDA agrees with Applicant's provided list of study endpoints. A detailed description of the primary and key secondary endpoints are provided below.
L777: ## ORR by RECIST and Tumor Volume Score (TVS):
L778: Best overall response of complete or partial response at Week-25 per RECIST 1.1 (as primary) and TVS (as key secondary) as assessed by blinded independent central review were included in this study. TVS is a semi-quantitative MRI scoring system that describes tumor mass and is based on 10% increments of the estimated volume of the maximally distended synovial cavity or tendon sheath involved.
L779: ## Active ROM:
L780: ROM is designed to measure movement of joints in unit of degrees. Active and passive ROM was assessed using a goniometer. Active is defined as measuring a motion produced by the participant's voluntary, unassisted muscle contraction, whereas passive is defined as measuring a motion produced by the participant of external force by an examiner during assessments.
L781: There were two forms used to collect ROM data: 1) screening through the end of Part 1 visit (Week 25), which collected both affected and non-affected joint data, and 2) from the end of Part 1 visit through the rest of the trial, which only collected data on the affected joint. The data collected included the joint, side of body, motions assessed, and degrees measured (the start and end position of motion) for active and passive motion. For each specific joint, the relevant planes of measurement were delineated on the case report form. ROM assessments were conducted at screening, Cycle 4, end of Part 1 visit (Week-25), and end of treatment (EOT).
L782: The measurement (in degrees) of the affected joint was used to derive a relative ROM obtained through normalization to the measurement from a reference standard value provided by the American Medical Association (AMA) per motion and type (active or passive) (Gerhardt, 2002). At Screening, the motion with the smallest relative ROM value (worst) was identified, and this motion was used for evaluating the change in relative ROM. Only the motion with the most impaired ROM at screening was selected for subsequent analyses. If there were ties, the multiple motions with the same relative ROM value at screening were identified, and the average of the relative ROM values were calculated at each post-Screening visit and used in the analysis as the single relative ROM value for that specific visit. The main analysis of relative ROM was based on the active measurement relative to a reference standard.
L783: Change from baseline to Week-25 in active ROM was included as a key secondary endpoint.
L784: ## **PROMIS-PF:**
L785: PROMIS Physical Function PROMIS-PF) is an item bank that consists of patient reported outcome (PRO) instrument designed to assess patients' perspectives on their physical functioning, including functioning based on use of one's upper extremities (dexterity), lower extremities (walking or mobility), and central regions (neck, back), as well as instrumental activities of daily living (IADLs). For MOTION, the Applicant selected 15 items to measure tumor location-specific physical function (referred to as upper and lower extremity scales). The lower extremity scale includes 13 items and is intended for use by participants with lower extremity tumors (e.g., knee, ankle, hip, etc.). The upper extremity scale includes 11 items and is intended for use by participants with upper extremity tumors (e.g., digits, hand, elbow, etc.). Nine items overlap between the two scales. Each item uses a 5-point verbal rating scale ranging from 1 ("Unable to do") to 5 ("Without any difficulty"). The recall period is unspecified. The PROMIS Physical items were administered electronically (via Clinical Ink Engage mobile application) at Week 1 Day 7 during screening period, Week 3 Day 1 during Cycle 1, Week 1 Day 1 and Week 2 Day 6 during Cycle 2 through Cycle 6, and end of Part 1.
L786: The table below shows the subset of 15 questions from PROMIS-PF item bank that were selected for inclusion in the assessment of functioning of upper and lower extremities as it related to tumor location.
L787: **Table 22: FDA-PROMIS Scoring Based on Tumor Location**
L788: Lower	Upper	Question
x	x	Are you able to push open a heavy door?
x	x	Are you able to exercise for an hour?
x	x	Are you able to carry a heavy object (over 10 pounds/5kgs)?

## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
