L791: Source: Table-3 from Statistical analysis plan document for MOTION study
L792: The 15-item PROMIS® Physical Function measure generates a total score that ranges from 0 to 100, where higher scores indicate better physical function. This score uses a T-score metric with a mean of 50 and standard deviation (SD) of 10 for a relevant reference population (i.e., U.S. general population).
L793: According to the Applicant, the scoring of the PROMIS Physical Function score used item-level calibrations via the Assessment Center Application Programming Interface (API), and no item level imputation was to be made for the PROMIS Physical Function scores based on the response pattern scoring.
L794: If a participant answered at least half of the questions for their tumor location, then the T-score generated based on the API scoring manual was used for analysis and those questions with missing responses were ignored for scoring. If a participant answered fewer than half of the questions for their tumor location, then the T-score was calculated and presented in listings but was not used for analysis.
L795: ## **Brief Pain Inventory Short Form (BPI-SF):**
L796: Item 3 of the BPI-SF (Worst Pain NRS) is a single item in the 9-item BPI-SF, a PRO instrument. BPI-SF Item 3 is designed to assess pain intensity at its worst. This item is rated using an 11-point numeric rating scale ranging from 0 (“No pain”) to 10 (“Pain as bad as you can imagine”). The recall period is the previous 24 hours. The BPI-SF was administered on Day 1 through Day 7 at Week 1 and Week 2 during screening; Day 2 through Day 7 at Week 1; Day 1 through Day 6 at Week 2; Day 1, Day 3 through Day 7
L797: at Week 3 during Cycle 1; Day 1 through Day 7 at Week 1 through Week 3 during Cycle 2-6; and End of Part 1.
L798: BPI-SF Item 3 generates a single item score ranging from 0 to 10, where higher scores indicate greater pain intensity.
L799: A responder analysis based on the BPI Worst Pain NRS item and analgesic use was performed as primary analysis (referred to as BPI-30 response). The change in mean BPI Worst Pain NRS for responder assessment was assessed by comparing data collected during a 14-day period prior to the current visit with baseline values collected prior to the first dose of study drug. A minimum of 4 measurements out of the 14 days was required to compute the mean; otherwise, it was set to missing. A responder (BPI-30 response) was defined as a patient who:
L800: - experienced a decrease of at least 30% in the mean BPI Worst Pain NRS item and
- did not experience a 30% or greater increase in narcotic analgesic use.
L801: ## **Worst-Stiffness and EQ-5D-5L VAS:**
L802: FDA focused its review of COA data on the ROM, PROMIS-PF, and BPI-SF information. The PRO data supporting EQ-5D-5L VAS and Worst Stiffness NRS endpoints were not reviewed by FDA due to the following reasons:
L803: - FDA did not agree with the scoring algorithm used for the Worst Stiffness NRS endpoint. FDA recommended the Applicant to use the PRO data collected during the 14-consecutive-day period that is closest to C1D1. However, this approach was not used for MOTION and the Worst Stiffness was measured every alternate day starting from Cycle-1.
- The EQ-5D-5L VAS is a generic preference-based measure intended to provide health utility index values for use in economic analyses and lacks evidence of content validity for use in estimating clinical benefit.
L804: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L805: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L806: The original version of the statistical analysis plan (SAP) (v1.0) was approved on 21 Nov 2022. The SAP was amended, and SAP v2.0 was approved on 09 Aug 2023. No major changes from protocol planned analyses were introduced in the amended version of the SAP.
L807: Efficacy analyses were performed on the intent-to-treat (ITT) Set. The ITT Set consisted of participants who were randomized to a study treatment regimen.
L808: The preponderance of missing data for the key secondary efficacy endpoints of active ROM, PROMIS-PF, Worst Stiffness NRS, and EQ-VAS were evaluated. If there was more than 10% of missing data at Week 25 in any of the endpoints, then the impact of the missing data was explored for that endpoint and included, but not limited to, the following analyses:
L809: - Multiple imputation based on randomized treatment to mimic missing at random
- A Pattern Mixture Model approach where participants who discontinued treatment due to an AE on vimseltinib were imputed based on the missing not at random assumption and imputed as if they received placebo
L810: To control overall type I error, a hierarchical testing procedure was used. Statistical testing was performed for the analysis of primary and key secondary endpoints in the following order at a two-sided 0.05 alpha level for each:
L811: - ORR per RECIST v1.1 at Week 25
- ORR per TVS at Week 25
- Mean change from baseline in active ROM at Week 25
- Mean change from baseline in the PROMIS-PF score at Week 25
- Mean change from baseline in the Worst Stiffness NRS score at Week 25
- Mean change from baseline in EQ-VAS at Week 25
- Proportion of responders based on BPI-30 Worst Pain NRS score and narcotic analgesic use at Week 25
L812: The primary efficacy endpoint and key secondary efficacy endpoints were analyzed in participant subgroups defined as follows:
L813: - Tumor location (lower limb versus all other)
- Region (U.S. versus non-U.S.)
- Participants with disease located in large joints (shoulder, elbow, hip, or knee) versus small joints (joints other than the shoulder, elbow, hip, or knee)
- Participants with disease located in the knee
- Disease subtype (localized versus diffuse)
- Participants at sites in the EU region only
- Age (18 \le \text{age} &lt; 50, 50 \le \text{age} &lt; 65, \text{age} \ge 65)
- Gender (female versus male)
- Prior surgery (yes versus no)
L814: - Prior imatinib/nilotinib (yes versus no)
L815: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L816: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the statistical analysis plan for MOTION. Additional details regarding the statistical analysis plan in MOTION are provided below.
L817: ## **Sample size assumptions:**
L818: In this study, a total of 120 patients were planned to be randomized in a 2:1 ratio to vimseltinib and placebo arms. This sample size would provide 98% power to detect a statistically significant difference between treatment groups, assuming true ORRs of 35% and 5% in the vimseltinib arm and the placebo arm, respectively, using a two-sided Fisher's exact test at 5% type I error rate level.
L819: ## **Analysis Methods:**
L820: The assessments schedules and analysis methods for the primary and key secondary endpoints are listed below. Key secondary endpoints were tested hierarchically in the order listed in the table below to adjust for multiplicity in the hypothesis tests. For the key secondary endpoints that are based on change from baseline assessments, mixed model for repeated measurements (MMRM) with the sandwich estimator to estimate the variance-covariance matrix was used. Each of these models included fixed effects for treatment group, timepoint, treatment group by timepoint interaction, stratification factors (region and tumor location), and the baseline value of the corresponding endpoint. The different timepoints that were included in the model specific to each endpoint are included in the below table.
L821: **Table 23: FDA-Primary Analyses Methods for Key Endpoints**
L822: Efficacy Endpoint	Analysis method
ORR (RECIST 1.1 and TVS)	Stratified Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test
ROM (only affected joint)	MMRM
Timepoints: Weeks-13 and 25 (Day 1 of Cycles 4 and 7 resp)
PROMIS-PF	MMRM
Timepoints: Weeks 5, 9, 13, 17, 21, 25
Worst Stiffness NRS	MMRM
L823: Timepoints: Weeks 5, 9, 13, 17, 21, 25
EQ-5D-5L VAS	MMRM
BPI-30 Worst Pain
NRS	Fisher's Exact test
L824: MMRM: mixed model for repeated measurements
L825: Source: FDA produced table
L826: ## Protocol Amendments
L827: ## The Applicant's Description:
L828: The original protocol was approved on 15 Dec 2020. The protocol underwent 3 global amendments and 4 regional amendments. Modifications did not impact the integrity of the study or interpretation of the results.
L829: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L830: FDA outlines the key protocol amendments below. All amendments were activated.
L831: - Protocol Amendment 1, dated February 11, 2021:
Applicant responded to FDA comments from the EOP meeting January 12, 2021.Protocol updated to ensure case report forms specify the reason why surgical resection would lead to functional morbidity or impairment.
- Schedule of assessments clarified to note frequency of liver function assessment.
- Minor modifications were made regarding the PRO analyses.
- Amendment 2, March 7, 2022:
Applicant updated protocol to address regulatory concerns.Applicant removed requirement for serum creatinine \le 1.5 ULN.
- Applicant added dose interruption guidance regarding cardiotoxicity risk.
- Protocol updated to prohibit medications that are OCT2 substrates.
- Protocol updated to add prophylactic measures for photosensitivity.
- Safety monitoring procedures for Part 1 and Part 2 crossover were aligned.
- Amendment 3, May 31, 2023:
No patients were enrolled under Protocol Amendment 3 because enrollment was completed. Applicant updated protocol to move ORR per RECIST to secondary endpoint and categorize secondary endpoints into hierarchical (Key-Secondary) and non-hierarchical (Other secondary endpoints). Additionally, the Applicant updated the protocol in response to requests from regulatory authorities and the study's safety data
L832: monitoring committee to add:
L833: - Guidance for management of Grade 2 prolonged QTc.
- Creatinine phosphokinase (CPK) assessments to be conducted at a central laboratory.
- Guidance for dose interruptions for Grade \ge 3 CPK elevation.

L845: For MOTION there were a total of 261 investigators at clinical sites that enrolled study patients. Signed financial disclosures could not be located for 5 sub-investigators for MOTION.
L846: Financial forms for the 256 investigators from MOTION showed no disclosable financial interests or arrangements.
L847: For the 5 investigators in the MOTION study without signed financial disclosures, the Applicant made repeated attempts to contact these investigators; however, they were unable to locate them due to the investigators no longer being associated with the institution where the study was performed. The Applicant independently reviewed internal financial records and confirmed that there were no direct payments to any of these 5 investigators. See Section 19.2 for additional details.

L872: ## The Applicant's Position:
L873: There were more IPDs for the placebo treatment arm than the vimseltinib arm. The incidences of major protocol deviations were similar between the treatment groups.
L874: ## FDA's Assessment:
L875: The incidence of important protocol deviations (1.2%, 13%) was lower in the vimseltinib arm than in the placebo arm, respectively and the incidences of major protocol deviations were similar (76%, 85%) respectively, in both arms. In the vimseltinib treatment arm, there was one important protocol deviation, where a single patient did not log their doses of vimseltinib during Cycle 2 and did not receive 7 doses of vimseltinib during Cycle 4. In the placebo arm, there were no instances of patients in the treatment arm receiving wrong doses or taking inappropriate other medications. In the placebo arm, 3 patients received inappropriate other medications while enrolled, and one patient took improper doses of placebo during Cycle 1. It is unlikely that protocol deviations had a significant impact on efficacy analyses.

L901: Given that this is one of the stratification factors included in the primary analysis, all the analyses were performed using the rederived tumor location to assess the impact of misclassification. The analysis results remained unchanged with the use of the rederived tumor location variable.
L902: Some countries do not collect data on race and ethnicity; therefore, the results for these categories report high percentages of patients for whom these have not been captured. Data on racial distribution of TGCT is scarce in the literature. One study conducted in 83 patients with TGCT who received pexidartinib, showed that 65% of patients were White, 18% per Other. 8% were Black, and 1.2% were missing. There were no Native Americans or Native Hawaiian or other Pacific Islander reported (Lin 2024).
L903: There were a total of 11% of patients enrolled in the vimseltinib arm and 10% of patients enrolled in the placebo arm in the US. In Asia, there was only one (2.5%) patient enrolled in the placebo arm. In Europe, which included sites in France, Italy, Netherlands, Germany, Great Britain, Spain, Norway, Poland, and Switzerland, 69% of patients were enrolled in the vimsletinib placebo arm and 80% enrolled in the placebo arm. Lastly, Australia enrolled 15% and 5%, in the vimseltinib arm and placebo arm, respectively. The ORR per subgroup analysis (US vs. other) is displayed in Figure 11 below.

L915: ## The Applicant's Position:
L916: TGCT history was similar between treatment arms and is anticipated to be representative of the intended population.
L917: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L918: FDA has independently analyzed these data and agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Localized TGCT is more common than diffuse TGCT and the knee is the most common joint affected in patients with TGCT. While localized TGCT is more common, patients with diffuse TGCT are more likely to experience recurrence (Stacchiotti 2023). Thus, patients with localized TGCT are less likely to be eligible to receive vimseltinib.
L919: In MOTION, diffuse TGCT was more common in both the treatment arm (69%) and the placebo arm (70%), and joints affected were evenly distributed between treatment and placebo arms.
L920: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L921: ## Data:
L922: ## *Treatment Compliance*
L923: Mean (standard deviation [STD]) relative dose intensity (RDI) in the double-blind period was 81.25% (18.207%) for vimseltinib and 91.62% (11.226%) for placebo. Median (min, max) RDI in the double-blind period was 87.5% (14.6%, 102.1%) for vimseltinib and 95.8% (50.0%, 100.0%) for placebo.
L924: The median RDI for the on-study vimseltinib treatment period, including both double-blind and open-label, was 81.3%.
L925: ## *Prior and Concomitant Procedures, Medications, and Therapies*
L926: The majority of participants (74.0%) reported at least 1 prior TGCT surgery or procedure. In addition, 22.8% reported receiving a prior TGCT systemic therapy, which included imatinib (18.7%) and nilotinib (4.9%), and 8.9% reported prior TGCT radiation therapy.
L927: The majority of participants received at least 1 prior or concomitant medication. Common prior medications included paracetamol (25.4%) and ibuprofen (18.9%). Common concomitant medications in the double-blind period included paracetamol (43.4%) and ibuprofen (28.7%).
L928: ## *Rescue Medication Use*
L929: Not applicable.
L930: ## The Applicant's Position:
L931: Treatment compliance and RDI were similar between the vimseltinib and placebo arms. Prior TGCT surgeries and procedures were as expected for the study population.
L932: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L933: Prior TGCT surgeries or procedures were completed in 64 patients (77%) in the vimseltinib arm and 27 patients (68%) in the placebo arm. This is consistent with expected disease history in the study population. Prior systemic treatments (with no patients receiving prior treatment with CSF1R directed therapy) was similar in both groups.
L934: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L935: ## Data:
L936: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L937: The analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint in Study MOTION at Week 25 demonstrated a statistically significant difference in ORR between vimseltinib and placebo arms (Table 28 and Figure 10).
L938: The ORR was 40% (95% CI: 29%, 51%) in the vimseltinib arm compared with no responses (95% CI: 0%, 9%) in the placebo arm (p&lt;0.0001). The median DOR was not reached; DOR ranged from 2.5+ to 19.4+ months.

L953: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L954: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the primary endpoint results for ORR and the Applicant's position regarding the demonstration of statistically significant ORR difference between the arms. FDA also considers the difference in ORR to be clinically meaningful and to represent an acceptable primary endpoint in this context.
L955: Subgroup analyses results of ORR at Week 25 per IRR in the ITT analysis set are presented in the forest plot below. These subgroups results are considered exploratory. The efficacy results in subpopulations with small sample size should be interpreted with caution. In the subgroups with adequate sample size, such as sex, age, ECOG

L1021: Vimseltinib was associated with a higher improvement from baseline in active ORR per TVS, ROM per AMA standard, PROMIS-PF, Worst Stiffness, and EQ-5D-5L VAS at Week 25 across nearly all subgroups. L1022: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1023: All key secondary endpoints assessed at Week 25 were met and were statistically significant. All key secondary endpoints demonstrated clinically meaningful improvement from baseline at Week 25. Results from subgroup analyses with adequate sample size were consistent with those from key secondary endpoint analyses.
L1024: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1025: FDA's assessment of each of the primary and key secondary endpoints are provided below.
L1026: ## ORR at Week-25 by RECIST 1.1:
L1027: FDA agrees with the Applicant's interpretation of the clinically and statistically significant difference between the treatment arms with respect to the ORR by RECIST 1.1 at Week 25.
L1028: ## ORR at Week-25 by TVS:
L1029: The analysis of ORR per TVS demonstrated a statistically significant difference in ORR per TVS between vimseltinib and placebo arms. This endpoint has not been validated as a surrogate for any of the established endpoints for clinical benefit claims in oncology, and therefore, FDA considered the results of the analysis of ORR by TVS as supportive of the anti-tumor activity with vimseltinib. The FDA review team thus included a sentence describing these results in Section 14 of product labeling, albeit after the table presenting the key efficacy results of MOTION.
L1030: FDA's evaluation of the selected COA/PRO endpoints as presented in Table 32. The results for EQ-5D-5L VAS and Worst Stiffness NRS are not included in this assessment as described
L1031: previously. The results of the active ROM, PROMIS-PF, and BPI-30 response at Week 25 analyses are included in Section 14 of product labelling as a table to present the key secondary efficacy results of MOTION.
L1032: ## Active ROM:
L1033: The data available rates for active ROM are provided in Figure 13. Overall, only 13% of assessments were missing at Week 25 compared to baseline. FDA considers this missingness as not substantial, and therefore, considers the observed magnitude of effect appears to be reliable.
L1034: **Figure 13: FDA-Available Data Rates for Active ROM up to Week-25**
L1035: *Source: Applicant's response to FDA Information request, dated 9/18/2024.*
L1036: The analysis of the active ROM demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in mean change from baseline ROM at Week 25 for vimseltinib compared to placebo, with an observed difference in least square (LS) means from placebo of 14.6% (95% CI: 4.0, 25.3; p=0.0077). The figure below presents the LS mean change from baseline plot of active ROM at Week-25.

The patients treated with vimseltinib demonstrated a greater improvement in change from baseline to Week 25 in active ROM. Some patients on the vimseltinib and on the placebo arms who did not have a tumor response had an improvement in ROM. This figure was included in Section 14 of product labeling.

L1042: The Applicant conducted additional sensitivity analysis (Table 32) to assess the impact of missing data in active ROM. In this sensitivity analysis, the missing data were imputed based on the mean value from patients who received placebo using multiple imputation. Based on this sensitivity analysis, the differences in LS mean change from baseline at Week 25 remained similar in magnitude for active ROM (difference in LS mean [95% CI] was 13.0% (2.7%, 23.3%). In addition to this, the Applicant performed additional sensitivity analyses using tipping point analyses to address the concern of informative missingness for the COA endpoints. The results of these sensitivity analysis along with the primary analysis are provided in the table below.

L1048: These sensitivity analyses support the primary analysis conclusions. The delta-adjusting pattern mixture model (PMM) analysis showed that the required penalty for loss of statistical significance was an average worsening of 24% points for patients with missing data in the vimseltinib arm. FDA concludes that the tipping point of 24% to be an extreme scenario and is unlikely.
L1049: Some patients who were non-responders (as measured by RECIST) to vimseltinib also had improved ROM, and some patients who received placebo also had improved ROM. This points to an incomplete overlap between the ORR measured radiographically and the demonstrated change in ROM. This may be due to several factors, including a placebo effect of being on a perceived effective therapy. However, these incongruous results (i.e., discordance on the vimseltinib arm between RECIST response and ORR, and improved ROM on the placebo arm in the absence of response) were fairly uncommon on both arms overall and do not overall jeopardize reliability/interpretability of ROM results.

L1062: Following treatment with vimseltinib, the results of the PROMIS-PF (through self-administered questionnaires) demonstrated a clinically meaningful improvement on the impact of the disease on patients' ability to perform daily activity. L1063: ## BPI-30 Response:
L1064: Available data rates and reasons for missing data of BPI-30 are presented in Figure 18 below. The available data rates remained above 77% in both arms which was considered to be sufficient to interpret the observed results.
L1065: **Figure 18** FDA-Available Data Rates for Active BPI up to Week-25
L1066: Source: Applicant's response to FDA Information request, dated 9/18/2024.
L1067: The stratified CMH test was used to compare BPI-30 response at Week 25 between the 2 treatment arms. A statistically significant improvement in BPI-30 response rates was observed with a difference of 26.2% (95% CI: 9.5, 42.8; p=0.0056).
L1068: As part of the sensitivity analysis for BPI-30, the following aspects were assessed:
L1069: ## (i) Impact of analgesic use
L1070: Overall, only &lt;5% patients (of n=123 patients enrolled on both arms) had an increase in narcotic analgesic use. The BPI-30 response rates remained the same irrespective of the amount of analgesic use.
L1071: ## (ii) Assessment period of 14 days
L1072: The average worst pain on day-1 of each cycle was calculated using the pain assessments obtained during the Weeks-2 and 3 of the prior cycle. At least 4 out of this 14-day assessment period were required to compute the average worst pain value. This 14-day assessment period and the minimum number of assessments (4 out of 14) has been further evaluated to assess the impact of these criteria on BPI-30 response rate results. Two sensitivity analyses were performed by considering.
L1073: - at least 4 assessments during only Week-3 (SA-1)
- at least 4 assessments in Week-2 and at least 4 assessments during Week-3 (SA-2).
L1074: Both the sensitivity analyses results did show a difference between the arms in terms of BPI-Response rates.
L1075: ## (iii) Impact of 30% change defined as part of responder definition:
L1076: As per the literature (Farrar, 2010) a 30% decrease in worst pain is generally considered equivalent to a raw reduction of two points on average in original scale and therefore, the Applicant considered this 30% reduction in pain as part of the responder definition. The results were significant based on this definition. To assess this assumption, FDA requested the Applicant to perform a sensitivity analysis of BPI-worst pain using the original values as recorded. The section below discusses the results based on these analyses.
L1077: Responder analyses using BPI-SF worst pain score with 2-point as the cut-off:
L1078: The table below presents the number of patients who were considered as responders based on the 2-point change in the average BPI Worst Pain at Week 25. Responder analysis using the 2-point cutoff shows that the response per BPI Worst Pain in the vimseltinib arm is higher than the responses observed in the placebo arm. Based on these results, the magnitude of difference between the arms was similar to that of the primary analysis results.

L1087: BPI Worst Pain (change from baseline to Week 25 based on the original reported values) was further assessed using the MMRM model. LS mean difference of -1.6 (95% CI: -2.3, -0.8) for BPI worst pain NRS was observed at Week 25.
L1088: From a clinical perspective, BPI assesses the severity of pain and its impact on daily functioning. It measures the impact of pain on daily function, location of pain, pain medications, and amount of pain relief. Pain relief is an important component of improvement in quality of life for patients with TGCT. The results of the BPI-30 demonstrate a clinically meaningful impact in improvement in pain in patients who received vimseltinib over placebo. This is included in product labeling.
L1089: Overall, the 15-item PROMIS measure and BPI Worst Pain results are supportive of the ORR and ROM data. Specifically, the data quality was sufficient at Week 25, and there was an early, sustained, and statistically significant (at Week 25) difference observed between vimseltinib and placebo. Interpretation of these results is limited by the clinically meaningful change thresholds as described in detail below regarding PROMIS-PF.

L1100: ## *Duration of Response*
L1101: Median DOR for participants with an objective response by RECIST and TVS at Week 25 was not reached as of 22 Aug 2023 data cutoff (DOR by RECIST range in months [0.03+, 11.7+]; DOR by TVS range in months [0.03+, 13.9+]) as responses remain ongoing.
L1102: An updated analysis of DOR (data cutoff 22 Feb 2024) was performed and is presented in Table . The median DOR for patients with TGCT with an objective response at Week 25 by RECIST v1.1 and TVS was not reached (DOR by RECIST and TVS range in months 2.5+, 19.4+), as responses are ongoing.
L1103: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1104: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1105: The response for all vimseltinib-treated participants in the double-blind and open-label periods was consistent with that seen in the vimseltinib treatment arm in the double-blind period.
L1106: Data indicate that patients with TGCT have demonstrated durable responses to vimseltinib.
L1107: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1108: FDA agrees with Applicant's position. See details of DOR in Section 8.1.2 subsection Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses).
L1109: ## Dose/Dose Response

L1112: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L1114: ## Durability of Response
L1115: ## Data:
L1116: See DOR in "Other Secondary Endpoints" above.
L1117: Updated analysis of MOTION DOR is provided with this submission, with approximately 49 weeks of follow-up, which showed responses to vimseltinib per RECIST v1.1 at Week 25 were ongoing (Table ).
L1118: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1119: Responses were long lasting.
L1120: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1121: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment, based on the data provided, that the response is durable in patients that received vimseltinib; however, in the vimseltinib arm, the median DOR was not yet reached; DOR ranged from 2.5+ to 19.4+ months. There were no responders in the placebo arm. See details of DOR in Section 8.1.2 subsection Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses).
L1122: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L1123: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1124: No long-term efficacy data, with the exception of those presented in the preceding sections, are available at the time of this application.
L1125: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1126: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Refer to FDA's assessment of Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Section 8.1.2) for additional details. During the Type B pre-NDA meeting on January 16, 2024, FDA requested an additional 6 months of follow-up duration data from the Applicant which was submitted with the original NDA submission. The additional 6 months of follow-up data had a higher percentage of patients with a \ge 6 months (85%) and \ge 9 months (58%) duration of response.
L1127: ## Data:
L1128: The 'Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints' sections above provide details of the COA endpoints of ROM, physical function, worst stiffness, and BPI-30.
L1129: The minimal clinically important difference (MCID) threshold for active ROM, PROMIS-PF, and Worst Stiffness NRS were determined based on a subset of the participants reaching end of Part 1 using blinded data. The MCID value for EQ-VAS was based on a change of 7 points based on published literature (Pickard et al, 2007). Distribution- and anchor-based methods were used to calculate potential thresholds based on feedback from the FDA. Distribution-based methods included examining one-half and one-quarter of the baseline standard deviation in the corresponding measure. Anchor-based methods examined the corresponding measure based on how participants/Investigators responded to the "anchor" questionnaire. Anchor questionnaires included patient global impression of severity (PGIS-PF), patient global impression of change (PGIC-PF), PGIC overall, clinical global impression of severity-ROM, clinical global impression of change-ROM, PGIS-ROM, and PGIC-ROM. The final proposed threshold was triangulated using the potential distribution and anchor-based thresholds and informed by the results of the qualitative interviews of the participants conducted within 28 days before the End of Part 1 study visit. These qualitative interviews were part of a cross-sectional substudy embedded into the MOTION study. The responder definitions that were determined using this method is robust and contributes to interpretation of clinically meaningful difference in patient-reported outcome (PRO) scores between the treatment arms.
L1130: Missing data for any of the COAs was minimal, and there were no meaningful differences in the rates of missing data between treatment arms. In a separate sensitivity analysis, if the missing data for all participants were imputed based on the mean value
L1131: using multiple imputation for participants that received placebo, then the differences in LS mean change from baseline at Week 25 remained significant for all PRO measures (Table 32. The results demonstrate robustness to the conclusion that participants receiving vimseltinib had statistically significant greater improvement in active ROM, PROMIS-PF, Worst Stiffness NRS, and EQ-5D-5L VAS compared to those receiving placebo, regardless of the true underlying missing data mechanism.
L1132: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1133: ROM, physical function, stiffness, and pain are concepts that are important and meaningful to patients with TGCT and the clinician-reported outcome and PRO measures in MOTION are considered fit-for-purpose assessments of these concepts. Vimseltinib treatment resulted in significant and meaningful improvements based on predetermined MCIDs in function as measured by active ROM and PROMIS-PF; in symptomatic improvements as measured by Worst Stiffness NRS and Worst Pain NRS; and in QoL as measured by EQ-VAS.
L1134: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1135: The meaningful change thresholds for active ROM assessment, 15-item PROMIS-PF, and Worst Stiffness NRS were determined based on a subset of the participants reaching the end of Part 1 (Week 25) using blinded data. Distribution- and anchor-based methods triangulated with patient exit interviews were used to calculate potential thresholds.
L1136: FDA recommends the use of anchor-based methods to establish meaningful within-patient change in COA-based endpoint scores, although other methods (e.g., qualitative evidence) can be used. Distribution-based methods are considered supportive to anchor-based methods.
L1137: Based on the submitted qualitative data, FDA agrees that the active ROM assessment (as measured by a goniometer) and the 15-item PROMIS-PF assesses relevant and important aspects of TGCT (i.e., active ROM and physical function, respectively).
L1138: FDA was unable to fully evaluate the reliability of the active ROM assessment due to insufficient evidence provided by the Applicant. While the Applicant used multiple anchors to conduct anchor-based analyses, the anchors have several limitations that impact the interpretability of the results of the anchor-based analyses. Despite the limitations of the anchors, the FDA evaluated the distributions of the primary anchor (i.e., PGIS-ROM item score) at baseline and Week 25. However, the sample size is small with some missing data (24.4%) at Week 25 for PGIS-ROM. Therefore, the distributions of the PGIS-ROM score at baseline and Week 25 needs to be interpreted with some caution. Based on this data, as well as the limitations in the PGIS-ROM and PGIC-ROM items, it is difficult to interpret the anchor-based analyses results. As such, it is unknown what
L1139: constitutes a meaningful change in the ROM assessment and FDA cannot agree with the Applicant's proposed meaningful change threshold in the active ROM assessment. However, the magnitude of difference between arms, statistical significance, and results from other endpoints including ORR and PROs provide supportive evidence that these ROM results may be clinically relevant and meaningful.
L1140: Altered range of motion can significantly impact a person's quality of life. In patients with TGCT, this can often lead to pain, stiffness, swelling, and limited range of motion leading to a diminished quality of life. Many times, previous treatment, including surgery, can worsen ROM over time, especially for those patients with dTGCT. The results for ROM demonstrate a clinically meaningful impact on patients who received vimseltinib versus placebo despite the limitations described above and inclusion of ROM results in product labeling was appropriate. The ROM results translate into an improvement in active ROM and support the ORR results. Overall, patients treated with vimseltinib had improved ROM, with a higher overall degree of improved ROM demonstrated in the patients treated with vimseltinib compared to placebo.
L1141: FDA cannot make a definitive conclusion whether the 15-item PROMIS PF measure is well-understood and interpreted appropriately in this context due to the limited approach used by the Applicant to evaluate participant comprehension.
L1142: The Applicant did not propose a target anchor change category based on the PGIS-Physical Function item. The Applicant provided insufficient evidence to support the target anchor change category on the PGIC-Physical Function based on the approach used in their exit interview study. Refer to the full COA review. To take a more conservative approach, FDA recommends using a 2-category change on the PGIS-Physical Function item and the "Much improved" response category on the PGIC-Physical Function item as the target anchor change category. Based on this, patients who experienced a 2-category change in the PGIS-Physical Function item had a mean change in the 15-item PROMIS Physical Function score of 7.3 and a median change of 6.5. Patients who reported "Much improved" in the PGIC-Physical Function item had a mean change in the 15-item PROMIS Physical Function score of 4.9 and a median change of 5.0.
L1143: The Applicant additionally inspected the eCDF curves for change from baseline to Week 25 in 15-item PROMIS Physical Function score by change in the PGIS-Physical Function and PGIC-Physical Function items. However, the graphs were difficult to interpret due to the small sample size at Week 25 (e.g., poor distribution across response categories).
L1144: FDA was unable to determine with confidence a threshold of clinically meaningful change in the 15-item PROMIS Physical Function score based on the submitted anchor-based analyses. However, as seen in Figure 21 change from baseline in the 15-item
L1145: Physical Function score showed adequate separation between the vimseltinib and placebo arm across a range that likely includes a clinically meaningful change threshold. A plausible clinical meaningful change threshold range in the 15-item PROMIS PF score could be between 3 and 7-points. FDA determined that despite limitations as described above, inclusion of PROMIS Physical Function results in product labeling was appropriate.
L1146: **Figure 21** FDA-eCDF Plot of the Change in PROMIS-PF Score from Baseline to Week 25 by Treatment Arm
L1147: ITT = Intent-to-Treat.
Baseline is defined as the most recent non-missing measurement prior to the first administration of study drug.
Week 25 includes results within 7 days prior and up to 2 days after to End of Part 1 visit.
The data used to develop this CDF plot includes those participants with PROMIS-PF data at Baseline and Week 25. This approach differs from the responder endpoint analysis which defined those with missing data as non-responders

L1149: FDA does not agree with how the Worst Stiffness NRS scores was analyzed as part of the endpoint. In the Type C Meeting Minutes dated May 22, 2023. FDA recommended the Applicant to use the PRO data collected during the 14-consecutive-day period that is closest to C1D1. However, this approach was not used for MOTION. See Section 8.1.2 Subsection Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints for additional FDA review of ROM.

L1154: FDA agrees no additional analyses were conducted.
L1155: ## 8.1.3. DCC-3014-01-001 (Phase 1/2 Supportive Study)
L1156: ## Trial Design
L1157: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1158: DCC-3014-01-001 (Phase 1/2 study) is an ongoing open-label Phase 1/2 study of vimseltinib in participants with advanced tumors. The cutoff for the efficacy data presented in this review is 27 Jun 2023.
L1159: The study comprised 2 distinct parts: dose escalation (which enrolled both participants with MST and participants with TGCT [Phase 1]) and expansion (which enrolled participants with TGCT only [Phase 2]).
L1160: The Dose Escalation Phase design is provided in Figure 22. Dose escalation was based on a pharmacologically guided 3+3 study design in participants with MST and TGCT. The maximum tolerated dose (MTD) was defined as the highest dose level at which no more than 1 of 6 dose limiting toxicity (DLT)-evaluable participants (&lt;33%) experienced a DLT(s) in Cycle 1 during dose escalation. The recommended phase 2 dose (RP2D) may be the MTD or a biologically active or maximally feasible dose that is lower than the MTD.
L1161: Figure 22 Schematic Illustration of Dose Escalation, Phase 1/2 Study
L1162: Abbreviations: MTD=maximum tolerated dose; N=sample size; PK=pharmacokinetics; QD=once daily; RP2D=recommended Phase 2 dose; TGCT=tenosynovial giant cell tumor.
L1163: Note: Cohorts 3 and 4 were run simultaneously. Dose escalation continued by increasing the total dose in the first cycle up to 50% from that set in the previous cohort. An additional lower or intermediate dose level(s) and/or alternate dose schedule(s) could be explored to determine an RP2D in participants with TGCT. More than one cohort at lower dose levels could be run simultaneously. Additional participants could be enrolled to a Dose Escalation Cohort for further evaluation of safety, efficacy, PK, and pharmacodynamics.
L1164: Upon determination of the RP2D, 2 expansion cohorts of participants with TGCT were initiated for further evaluation of safety, tolerability, efficacy, PK, pharmacodynamics, and efficacy to support any future study of vimseltinib in this population (Cohort A and Cohort B).
L1165: All participants in the Dose Expansion Phase received vimseltinib at the RP2D (30 mg twice weekly on Day 1 and Day 5 with no loading dose at the same time each week). A
L1166: data monitoring committee monitored the safety and efficacy data in both phases of the study on a periodic basis to ensure the ongoing safety of study participants.
L1167: The study consisted of a screening period conducted within 28 days (Dose Escalation Phase) or 42 days (Expansion Phase) prior to the first dose of study drug, a treatment period of 28-day cycles, an end of treatment visit, and a Follow-up Safety visit 30 days (\pm5 days) after the last dose of study drug. Participants were then followed in the Disease Follow-up period for up to 2 years after last dose of study treatment or until radiological progression, start of new subsequent therapy/surgery, or withdrawal of consent.
L1168: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1169: Based on the information presented by the Applicant, the reviewer agrees with Applicant's description of the supportive study. The maximum administered dose of vimseltinib in patients with TGCT was 30 mg twice weekly following a 30 mg x 5 day loading dose. All patients in the Dose Expansion Part received vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly on Day 1 and Day 5 with no loading dose. A total of 66 patients with TGCT received the RP2D of 30 mg twice weekly. An additional 32 patients with TGCT were treated at other doses.
L1170: ## Eligibility Criteria
L1171: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1172: The Dose Escalation Phase enrolled male or female adult participants (\ge18 years of age)
L1173: - with advanced MST that had progressed after treatment with all available therapies known to confer clinical benefit or for which conventional therapy was not considered effective as judged by the Investigator, or
- with histologically confirmed diagnosis of TGCT. A tumor biopsy to confirm TGCT diagnosis was required if no histology/pathology was available at the time of screening.
L1174: The Dose Expansion Phase enrolled male and female adult participants (\ge18 years of age)
L1175: - with histologically confirmed diagnosis of TGCT, a tumor biopsy to confirm TGCT diagnosis was required if no histology/pathology was available at the time of screening,
- with disease for which surgical resection would potentially cause worsening of functional limitation or severe morbidity, as determined by surgical consultation or a multidisciplinary tumor board, or
L1176: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1177: - with symptomatic disease with at least moderate pain per BPI Worst Pain or at least moderate stiffness per Worst Stiffness NRS item (defined as a score of 4 or more, with 10 describing the worst condition) within 30 days of the first dose documented in the medical record.
L1178: Participants in Expansion Cohort A had not received prior anti-CSF1 or anti-CSF1R treatment, with the exception of imatinib or nilotinib. Participants in Dose Expansion Cohort B had received prior systemic treatment with anti-CSF1 or anti-CSF1R therapy.
L1179: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1180: The Applicant highlighted the key inclusion criteria. The following are important aspects of the inclusion criteria:
L1181: - Dose expansion phase required TGCT patients to have disease for which surgical resection would potentially cause worsening morbidity or functional limitations and symptomatic disease with at least moderate pain or stiffness.
- AST/ALT \le 1.5 times upper limit of normal (which was amended over time).
L1182: The following patients were not enrolled:
L1183: - Expansion Cohort A: previous use of systemic therapy targeting CSF1 or CSF1R; previous therapy with imatinib and nilotinib is allowed.
- Expansion Cohort B: previous discontinuation of anti-CSF1 or anti-CSF1R due to drug-induced liver injury.
L1184: The reviewer notes that the a few key eligibility criteria for DCC-3014-01-001 differ from MOTION. In the original DCC-3014-01-001 study, patients were required to have bilirubin \le 1.5 times the upper limit of normal (ULN); AST/ALT \le 3 \times ULN to be eligible to enroll. Throughout the course of the study, amendments to the protocol were made to reflect the possibility of hepatotoxicity. With protocol amendment 5, patients with TGCT were eligible if bilirubin \le 1.5 times the ULN; and AST/ALT \le 1.5 \times ULN. In protocol amendment 6, patients with TGCT had to have bilirubin, AST/ALT \le ULN and patients with active liver or biliary disease including evidence of fatty liver, nonalcoholic steatohepatitis (NASH), or cirrhosis were excluded. Within the original MOTION protocol, these stricter parameters for eligibility were already in place.

L1201: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1202: FDA agrees with Applicant's descriptions of endpoints. TGCT is a slow-growing progressive and debilitating but non-lethal disease. ORR can be used to measure the reduction in tumor burden over time. Time to event endpoints such as progression-free survival and overall survival and PRO endpoints are not interpretable in a single-arm study.

L1208: ## Protocol Amendments
L1209: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1210: The original protocol was approved on 27 Sep 2016. No participants were enrolled under the original version of the protocol. The protocol underwent 6 global amendments and 4 regional amendments.
L1211: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1212: FDA outlines the 6 protocol amendments below. All amendments were activated.
L1213: 1. Amendment 1, December 13, 2016: Protocol updated to incorporate comments made
L1214: by FDA during 30-day review of initial IND application.

- Protocol added an abbreviated 3+3 dose escalation phase for patients with hematologic malignancies.
- Protocol clarified that enrollment of patients with hematologic malignancies would be based on the RP2D determined in the escalation phase.
- Dose limiting criteria and dose interruption rules were updated.
- Amendment 2, August 25, 2017: Protocol updated to amend dose escalation schedule and AE reporting period based on preliminary half-life of DCC-3014.Dose escalation scheme was amended to include possible loading/maintenance dosing schedules.
- Dose interruptions amended to allow for patients with asymptomatic Grade \ge 3 elevation in liver transaminases in the absence of bilirubin elevation to continue to receive vimseltinib.
- Protocol amended to note that adverse events should be reported through 75 days of the last dose of vimseltinib (previously 30 days) due to new information regarding drug half-life.
- Amendment 3, December 20, 2018: Hematologic malignancy cohort was replaced with a solid tumor cohort and a DTGCT cohort.An objective was added to assess anti-tumor activity in patients with DTGCT.
- Age requirement for patients with DTGCT was set to \ge 16 years old.
- New schedule of assessment was added for patients with solid tumors and patients with TGCT.
- Amendment 4, August 21, 2019: Protocol updated to include additional objectives for evaluation of anti-tumor activity in patients with dTGCT.RECIST v1.1 assessment was added as a primary objective.
- Additional primary, secondary and exploratory endpoints for dTGCT were added.
- Dose limiting toxicities were updated.
- Amendment 5, March 25, 2020: Protocol modified to broaden inclusion of TGCT population to both localized and diffuse.Expansion cohort updated to include only patients with TGCT.
- Cohort A redefined for patients with TGCT without prior anti-CSF1/CSF1R treatment.
- Amendment 6, February 16, 2021: Protocol updated to modify eligibility for TGCT and update dose modification/guidance.Disease follow-up period for all patients with TGCT was added for up to 2 years after last dose of study treatment, until radiological progression, surgery,
L1215: or other therapy is given.

b. Dose escalation and expansion phase assessment schedule as modified to reduce patient burden with fewer study visits after 12 months of study treatment.

L1255: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L1256: ## Data:

No participant had known metastatic disease (99.0% no metastatic disease, 1.0% unknown).

L1267: ## *Concomitant Medications*
L1268: The majority of participants with TGCT reported at least one prior or concomitant medication and/or non-drug therapy. Frequently reported prior medications included paracetamol (18.6% of participants) and ibuprofen (14.4%). Common concomitant
L1269: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1270: medications included paracetamol (45.4% of participants), ibuprofen (28.9%), furosemide (17.5%), tozinameran (14.4%), and naproxen (11.3%).

L1272: Not applicable.

L1277: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L1278: ## Data:
L1279: Objective Response at Week 25 (C7D1) by IRR Using RECIST v1.1 (Expansion Cohort A)
L1280: In Cohort A (Expansion Phase, 30 mg twice weekly), ORR at Week 25 (C7D1) as assessed by IRR using RECIST v1.1 was 38% (95% CI: 24%, 53%); 0 participants had achieved CR, and 17 had achieved PR. Results of a sensitivity assessment of investigator review using RECIST v1.1 were consistent with the primary analysis.
L1281: Duration of Response by IRR Using RECIST v1.1 (Expansion Cohort A)
L1282: During the entire treatment period to data cutoff, ORR in participants with TGCT in Cohort A was 64% (95% CI: 49%, 78%) by IRR using RECIST v1.1. Median DOR was not reached because all responses were ongoing at the time of analysis.
L1283: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1284: Vimseltinib demonstrated efficacy in participants with TGCT with no prior specific anti CSF1/CSF1R therapy (except imatinib or nilotinib) at the RP2D.
L1285: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1286: FDA did not independently verify the efficacy results of this study, as the evaluation of efficacy in the proposed indication for vimseltinib is based on MOTION. In DCC-3014-01-001, 97 patients with TGCT were treated with vimseltinib, including 65 patients treated at a dose of 30 mg twice weekly. The results presented by the Applicant
L1287: demonstrate that patients with TGCT who have not been previously treated with a targeted CSF1/CSF1R therapy (Cohort A) at a dose of 30 mg twice weekly, had an ORR of 38% at Week 25 and duration of response was not reached. The efficacy results are similar to the efficacy results in MOTION.
L1288: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1289: ## Data:
L1290: All participant data relating to the study was entered into the electronic CRFs unless transmitted to the sponsor or designee electronically (e.g., laboratory data). The investigator was responsible for verifying that data entries were accurate and correct by electronically signing the CRF. The investigator maintained accurate documentation (source data) that supported the information entered in the CRF. The investigator permitted study-related monitoring, audits, Institutional Review Board (IRB) / Independent Ethics Committee (IEC) review, and regulatory agency inspections and provided direct access to source data documents. Monitoring details describing strategy methods, responsibilities, and requirements, including handling of noncompliance issues and monitoring techniques (on-site, remote monitoring) were indicated in the monitoring plan to ensure the protocol and GCP was followed.
L1291: The sponsor or designee was responsible for the data management of this study including quality checking of the data. The sponsor assumed accountability for actions delegated to other individuals (e.g., Contract Research Organizations).
L1292: Study monitors performed ongoing source data verification to confirm that data entered into the eCRF by authorized site personnel were accurate, complete, and verifiable from source documents; that the safety and rights of participants were being protected; and that the study was being conducted in accordance with the currently approved protocol and any other study agreements, ICH GCP, and all applicable regulatory requirements. Records and documents, including signed ICFs, pertaining to the conduct of this study were retained by the investigator according to specifications in the ICH guidelines, local regulations, or as specified in the clinical trial agreement, whichever was longer. No records were to be destroyed during the retention period without the written approval of the sponsor. No records were to be transferred to another location or party without written notification to the sponsor. Deciphera conducts clinical studies according to procedures that incorporate the ethical principles of GCP. To ensure compliance with these procedures and to assess the adequacy of quality control procedures, Deciphera undertakes a GCP audit program. Audits were performed by Deciphera Quality Assurance (or designees) operating independently of the study monitors. The audits were aimed at study documentation, investigator sites, and CSRs. The audit program, together with Deciphera's internal quality control procedures, provide reassurance that study conclusions were based on valid procedures for data management and analysis, and that the clinical study program was carried out in accordance with GCP guidelines.

L1297: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1298: ## Data:
L1299: ## *Objective Tumor Response at Week 25 (C7D1) by IRR Using TVS (Expansion Cohort A)*
L1300: In Cohort A, ORR at Week 25 (C7D1) assessed by IRR using TVS was 51% (95% CI: 36%, 66%); 0 participants had achieved CR, and 23 had achieved PR. During the overall treatment period, ORR assessed by IRR using TVS was 64% (95% CI: 49%, 78%) in Cohort A; 0 participants experienced CR, and 29 experienced PR. Median DOR could not be estimated by Kaplan-Meier for TVS as no participants had progressed or died using TVS at the data cutoff.
L1301: ## *Functional Assessment (Expansion Cohort A)*
L1302: Active relative ROM across a variety of joints increased by 19.74 (STD: 43.483) percentage points from Baseline to Week 25 (C7D1) in participants in Cohort A.
L1303: ## *Patient-reported Outcomes (Expansion Cohort A)*
L1304: ## BPI Worst Pain Response (Expansion Cohort A)
L1305: In Cohort A, at Week 25 (C7D1) 50.0% (95% CI: 34.9%, 65.1%) of participants were considered BPI worst pain responders (i.e., experienced a decrease of at least 30% in the mean BPI worst pain item and did not experience a 30% or greater increase in narcotic analgesic use).
L1306: ## PROMIS-PF Questionnaire (Expansion Cohort A)
L1307: Mean (STD) improvement from Baseline in PROMIS-PF for all participants with TGCT in Cohort A was 3.9 (7.83) points at Week 25 (C7D1).
L1308: ## Worst Stiffness NRS (Expansion Cohort A)
L1309: Participants with TGCT in Cohort A showed a mean (STD) improvement in worst stiffness in the last 24 hours by NRS of 1.81 (2.676) points by Week 25 (C7D1).
L1310: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1311: Using TVS, ORR was 51.1% (23 participants with PR) at 30 mg twice weekly, in participants with TGCT with no prior specific anti-CSF1/CSF1R therapy (except imatinib or nilotinib).
L1312: Analysis of functional assessments showed a mean 19.74 percentage point increase in active relative ROM from baseline in participants with TGCT.
L1313: Analysis of PRO endpoints showed an improvement in worst pain based on BPI, physical function based on PROMIS-PF, and Worst Stiffness NRS.

L1319: ## 8.1.6. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials

The Phase 1/2 study included a Dose Escalation Phase and enrolled participants with other solid tumors. Therefore, only efficacy results from participants with TGCT who received the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly from this study are presented. This includes:
L1321: - Participants with TGCT and without prior anti-CSF1/CSF1R treatment (Cohort A; Phase 2 Dose Expansion)
- Participants with TGCT and with prior anti-CSF1/CSF1R treatment (Cohort B; Phase 2 Dose Expansion)
- Participants with TGCT who received vimseltinib loading dose (30 mg × 5 days) followed by 30 mg twice weekly (Cohort 5 TGCT; Phase 1 Dose Escalation)

L1347: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L1348: ## Data:
L1349: ## *Objective Response Rate per Tumor Volume Score at Week 25*
L1350: In MOTION, the ORR per TVS at Week 25 based on IRR was significantly higher in the vimseltinib arm compared with the placebo arm (67% versus 0%; p&lt;0.0001). Of the 56 participants in the vimseltinib arm who responded to treatment in MOTION, 4 (5%) had CR and 52 (63%) had PR.
L1351: The ORR per TVS at Week 25 based on IRR was high in both MOTION vimseltinib arm (67%) and Phase 1/2 Dose Expansion Cohort A (51%). The ORR per TVS at Week 25 by blinded IRR in Phase 1/2 Dose Escalation Cohort 5 was 38% and in Dose Expansion Cohort B was 29%.
L1352: ## *Duration of Response*
L1353: DOR per RECIST 1.1 and TVS for the MOTION double-blind period, primary analysis with a cutoff of 22 Aug 2023, are presented in Section 8.1.2. Tumor responses were long lasting based on ORR per RECIST v1.1 and per TVS with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly. A primary analysis from the double-blind part of MOTION (22 Aug 2023) and an updated analysis with 6 months of additional follow-up (22 Feb 2024) demonstrated long-lasting response with vimseltinib. The Kaplan-Meier estimated median DOR for responders to vimseltinib at Week 25 per RECIST v1.1 was not reached at either analysis, with responses lasting up to 84.4+ weeks and with all but 1 response ongoing at the time of the updated analysis (Table 38).
L1354: In the Phase 1/2 study, up to the data cutoff date (27 Jun 2023), the Kaplan-Meier estimated median DOR for responders to vimseltinib per RECIST v1.1 was not reached across all TGCT 30 mg twice weekly cohorts (Cohorts A, B, and 5), with responses lasting up to 196+ weeks; all responses were ongoing, and most responses were maintained for at least 12 months.
L1355: The updated analysis of MOTION DOR with a median nearly 49 weeks of follow-up was conducted at a similar median follow-up duration as the Phase 1/2 study (ranged from 48.7 weeks to 56.1 weeks) and similarly demonstrated ongoing responses with approximately 12 months of on-study treatment. Given participants in Phase 1/2 initiated treatment before MOTION and had more opportunity to receive treatment past Week 25, response duration in Phase 1/2 may be considered as an early indicator for patients who have responded to vimseltinib treatment during the MOTION study.
L1356: The DOR per TVS demonstrated a trend similar to those per RECIST v1.1.

L1367: ## *Functional and Patient-reported Endpoints*
L1368: Collectively, assessment of key secondary ROM and PRO endpoints at Week 25 in MOTION demonstrated statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement with vimseltinib treatment over placebo (Table 31). The change from baseline in ROM and PRO endpoints were consistent across the vimseltinib arm in MOTION and Phase 1/2 expansion cohorts.
L1369: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1370: Assessment of the key secondary endpoint ORR by TVS at Week 25 was planned to provide an early indicator of tumor response with vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly. The TVS measures volumetric changes in irregularly shaped tumors like TGCT, which may not be captured with single dimension measurements per RECIST v1.1 at a given time point (Tap et al, 2015). Thus, changes in TGCT size may be observed earlier with TVS than with RECIST v1.1.
L1371: ORR per TVS was high in both MOTION vimseltinib arm (67%) and Phase 1/2 expansion Cohort A (51%).
L1372: Among objective responders in the vimseltinib arm at Week 25 per RECIST v1.1, the median DOR was not reached at either the primary or updated analysis with additional 6 months of follow-up, with responses lasting up to 84.4+ weeks and with all but 1 response ongoing at the time of the updated analysis. The updated MOTION DOR data demonstrate prolonged durability of response in participants with TGCT with further follow-up.
L1373: MOTION met all key secondary endpoints demonstrating statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvements in TVS, ROM, physical function, worst stiffness, QoL, and worst pain compared to placebo. The totality of efficacy data demonstrates a robust, statistically significant, and clinically meaningful efficacy response. The efficacy and PRO responses in MOTION were consistent with what was observed in participants with TGCT who received vimseltinib at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly in the Phase 1/2 study.
L1374: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1375: FDA did not independently verify the results of DCC-01-001 presented here, as the efficacy of vimseltinib was based on MOTION. FDA agrees that based on the data presented here, ORR per TVS was similar in the MOTION study (67%) and Cohort A of DCC-3014-01-001.
L1376: PRO endpoints in a single-arm study are uninterpretable.

An additional subgroup analysis of ORR per RECIST v1.1 at Week 25 by primary joint location (knee, ankle, hip, all other) was also conducted. These results demonstrated consistent benefit across variables including tumor location, disease subtype, and prior treatment.

- Region (U.S. versus non-U.S.)
- Participants with disease located in large joints (shoulder, elbow, hip, or knee) versus small joints (joints other than the shoulder, elbow, hip, or knee)

L1389: ## 8.1.7. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1390: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1391: There is no formal integrated assessment of efficacy. The data from MOTION, supported by data from the Phase 1/2 study, demonstrate vimseltinib provides statistically significant and long-lasting tumor response based on ORR per RECIST v1.1 and ORR by TVS in participants with symptomatic TGCT. Vimseltinib treatment also resulted in significant and meaningful improvements based on predetermined MCIDs in function as measured by active ROM and PROMIS-PF; in symptomatic improvements as measured by Worst Stiffness NRS and Worst Pain NRS; and in QoL as measured by EQ-VAS. Responses were observed across subgroups including age, sex, tumor location, and prior therapies, suggesting generalizability in the intended patient population. The results support approval of vimseltinib for the treatment of adult patients with symptomatic at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly.
L1392: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1393: FDA agrees that despite no formal integrated assessment of efficacy, the data from MOTION are statistically and clinically significant to support approval and are consistent with the results observed in the supportive study DCC-3014-01-001 Cohort A, which was not independently confirmed by the FDA. The ORR in MOTION per RECIST v1.1 performed at Week 25 shows a clinically significant benefit to patients (MOTION: 40%, DCC-3014-01-001 Cohort A 30 mg Vimseltinib twice weekly in patients not pre-treated with anti-CSF1/CSF1R therapy: 38%). In MOTION, the median DOR was not reached, with a range of 2.5+ to 19.4+ months. A total of 28 (85%) patients experienced a DOR of \ge6 months and 19 (58%) patients experienced a DOR of \ge9 months. Additionally, patients experienced clinically meaningful improvements on key secondary endpoints, including change from baseline ROM of affected joint and change from baseline in the PROs (PROMIS-PF and BPI-30 response) which further supported the efficacy endpoint. Therefore, in addition to inclusion of ORR and DOR in product labeling, the review team also included results of these secondary endpoints.
L1394: The Applicant originally proposed an indication which stated that vimseltinib was indicated for adult patients with symptomatic TGCT. During review of the NDA submission, FDA amended the proposed indication to state that it is indicated for the treatment of patients with symptomatic TGCT for whom surgical resection will potentially cause worsening functional limitations or severe morbidity which more accurately reflects the patient population enrolled in MOTION. The Applicant agreed.
L1395: ROMVIMZA is indicated for treatment of adult patients with symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT).
L1396: ROMVIMZA is indicated for treatment of adult patients with symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT) for which surgical resection will potentially cause worsening functional limitation or severe morbidity.
L1397: These data support the efficacy of vimseltinib in the treatment of patients with TGCT.
L1398: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1399: ## Data:
L1400: Refer to Section 8.2.4 for summaries of deaths, SAEs, AEs leading to discontinuations, and other safety data.
L1401: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1402: Vimseltinib has a manageable and acceptable safety profile in participants with TGCT.
L1403: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1404: Although, FDA generally agrees with Applicant's assessment that vimseltinib has an overall manageable safety profile and an acceptable risk:benefit assessment, a known adverse reaction with another CSFRI1 inhibitor, pexidartinib is hepatotoxicity and pexidartinib has a BOXED Warning for this toxicity and is only available through a REMS program.
L1405: Cases of serious and fatal liver injury have occurred with the use pexidartinib. Therefore, FDA conducted a comprehensive review of hepatotoxicity with the use of vimseltinib. During FDA review, serious and fatal liver injury were not observed with vimseltinib. Cases of \le Grade 2 AST and ALT elevations were common in patients who received vimseltinib, though no patients developed bilirubin elevations. FDA did not identify any cases of Hy's law or drug-induced liver injury (DILI). Due to this known risk of in class hepatotoxicity, sections 2.2 and 5.1 of product labeling addresses specific guidelines for managing potential hepatotoxicity in patients, including guidelines for routine laboratory surveillance.
L1406: After multidisciplinary review of clinical and nonclinical data, FDA determined that a REMS program was not required for vimseltinib. However, FDA will continue to collect further data on hepatotoxicity through a PMR registry study enrolling at least 100 patients in the postmarketing setting. There is nonclinical evidence to suggest small molecule inhibitors of CSFRI1 lead to a higher incidence of elevated transaminases, but also may cause hepatic damage. FDA will continue to reevaluate the safety data for hepatotoxicity.
L1407: Additional warnings and precautions included in section 5.1 of product labeling are for potential embryo-fetal toxicity, allergic reactions to FD&amp;C Yellow No.5 (Tartazine) and No. 6 (Sunset Yellow FCF) and increased creatinine without affecting renal function.
L1408: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1409: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1410: The clinical review of safety of vimseltinib as a treatment for patients with symptomatic TGCT was based on the MOTION study (details of study design are in Section 8.1.1). The majority of the safety evaluation is based on the first 25 weeks of treatment to allow for comparison between the 83 participants randomized to vimseltinib and the 39 participants randomized to placebo.
L1411: The integrated safety database comprises data from participants included in the safety analysis set from the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION, including participants from MOTION originally randomized to vimseltinib and participants who crossed over to vimseltinib after receiving placebo. The safety analysis set consists of participants who have received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib.
L1412: The safety analysis set included the following populations:
L1413: - **Pool 1 (All participants with TGCT at 30 mg twice weekly dose):** All participants with TGCT in the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly are included. Data are summarized by Expansion Cohorts and MOTION (Cohort A, Cohort B, MOTION, Cohort A + MOTION) and overall (Cohort A + Cohort B + MOTION).Phase 1/2 Cohort A is comprised of participants with TGCT who are not amenable to surgery and who have not received prior CSF1 or CSF1R therapy (excluding imatinib and/or nilotinib); a population similar to that of the target patient population of the ongoing pivotal MOTION study. Thus, for the pooled analysis, Cohort A and MOTION are grouped together.
- Phase 1/2 Cohort B is comprised of participants with TGCT who were not amenable to surgery and who received prior CSF1 or CSF1R therapy and is presented separately in the pooled analysis.
- **Pool 2 (All participants with TGCT, any dose):** All participants with TGCT in the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib are included. Data are summarized by study phase (Escalation, Expansion, and MOTION) and overall.
- **Pool 3 (All participants, any dose):** All participants who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib in the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION regardless of tumor type or dose of vimseltinib are included. Data are summarized by tumor type (MST versus TGCT) and overall.
L1414: - **Pool 4 (All participants at 30 mg twice weekly dose):** All participants in the Phase 1/2 study and MOTION who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly, including those participants who received a loading dose. Data are summarized by tumor type (MST versus TGCT) and overall.
L1415: For all analysis pools, the analysis period comprises the duration from the first dose of vimseltinib through all available follow-up until the data cutoff (22 Feb 2024 and 27 Dec 2023 for MOTION and the Phase 1/2 study, respectively).
L1416: In order to better understand the safety of vimseltinib in the indicated population, a review of adverse events, laboratory assessments, vital signs, physical findings, and other observations related to safety was completed for Pool 1. A brief summary of data from participants in Pool 3 (including participants with MST) was also provided, as this is the largest safety pool.
L1417: In addition, 211 healthy participants received at least one dose of vimseltinib in the clinical pharmacology studies DCC-3014-01-002, DCC-3014-01-003, DCC-3014-01-004, and DCC-3014-01-006.
L1418: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1419: The review team agrees with the safety Pools as stated above were agreed upon in the preNDA meeting held on January 16, 2024. During the meeting, FDA stated that although the safety assessment would be based on all patients with any tumor type who received at least one dose of vimseltinib (Pool 3 n=253), the focus of safety would be on the 83 patients who received vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly in the double-blind portion of MOTION. The use of the larger data set was to assess any low incidence occurring serious adverse events and to contribute to the Warnings and Precautions section of the labeling. Section 6 of product labeling is thus based on the 83 patients who received vimseltinib in the double-blind portion of MOTION. Warnings and Precautions evaluated in product labeling was based on 253 patients who received any dose of vimseltinib in the double-blind portion of MOTION (n=83), the open-label portion of MOTION (n=35) and in DCC-3014-01-001 which included patients with TGCT as well as other solid tumors (n=135). These datasets were independently reviewed by the review team. The safety analysis within this section will mainly reflect the patients in the double-blind portion of MOTION unless otherwise stated.
L1420: The review team for did not independently evaluate the 211 healthy participants who received a single-dose of vimseltinib in the clinical pharmacology studies DCC-3014-01-002, DCC-3014-01-003, DCC-3014-01-004, and DCC-3014-01-006.

L1457: Exposure to study treatment was considered appropriate to allow for an adequate assessment of safety in participants who were representative of the intended target population.
L1458: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1459: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of treatment exposure for patients enrolled in the double-blind phase of MOTION in Table 40.
L1460: For clarification, the following define each patient population listed by the Applicant in Table 41.
L1461: - Cohort A: Patients with TGCT in the expansion phase of DCC-3014-01-001 with no prior anti-CSF1 or anti-CSF1R
- Cohort B: Patients with TGCT in the expansion phase of DCC-3014-01-001 no prior anti-CSF1 or anti-CSF1R
- MOTION: Patients who received at least one dose of vimseltinib in the double-blind (n=83) and in the open-label portion (n=35)
L1462: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L1463: ## Data:
L1464: Population characteristics were similar between treatment arms in the double-blind period of MOTION. The mean (STD) age of participants was 43.4 (13.80) years. The participants were generally White (65.0%), non-Hispanic (69.1%), and female (59.3%). The mean (STD) baseline BMI was 27.37 (5.974) kg/m2. Majority of participants were non-U.S. (89.4%) and presented with tumor in the lower limb (88.6%) as recorded per interactive response technology. L1481: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1482: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1483: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1484: The evaluation of safety is based on data from the Phase 3 pivotal study, MOTION, and Cohorts A and B of the supportive Phase 1/2 study. Further details are provided in Section 8.2.1. This population allows for a thorough assessment of the vimseltinib safety data and a judgment of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with TGCT. The duration of the treatment is adequate to allow assessment of adverse reactions over time. The safety database represents the sex, age, and race consistent with that observed in the population of patients with TGCT.
L1485: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1486: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the adequacy of the safety database. The primary safety analyses to support approval was based on the 83 patients who received vimseltinib in the double-blind portion of MOTION. An additional 135 patients treated on DCC-3014-01-001 and 35 patients who were treated with vimseltinib in the open-label portion of MOTION were evaluated, for a total of 253 patients who received at least one dose of vimseltinib.
L1487: ## 8.2.3. Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1488: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1489: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following a completion of site inspections; data in the case report forms and AE databases were consistent.
L1490: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1491: FDA agrees with Applicant's assessment.
L1492: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1493: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1494: A TEAE was defined as any AE that occurred or worsened after the administration of the first dose of vimseltinib and through 30 days after the last dose of vimseltinib or the day before the start of a new anti-tumor therapy. Drug-related AEs reported after 30 days following the last dose of vimseltinib were also considered as treatment-emergent.
L1495: Adverse events were classified using Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) v26.0.
L1496: The severity grade of the AEs for the Phase 1/2 study was assessed by National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event (NCI-CTCAE) v4.03 and for MOTION by NCI-CTCAE v5.0.
L1497: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1498: FDA agrees with the categorization of TEAEs and severity. The Applicant provided appropriate definitions and reporting requirements for AEs and serious AEs (SAEs). AE evaluation was performed at the time of informed consent up to 30 days after the last dose of study treatment.
L1499: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1500: ## Data:
L1501: Routine clinical tests, including vital signs, 12-lead ECG, hematology, chemistry, coagulation, and urinalysis, were performed according to the Schedules of Assessments in the study protocols for the MOTION and Phase 1/2 studies.
L1502: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1503: The tests and frequency of testing are considered adequate to assess the safety of vimseltinib in the MOTION and Phase 1/2 studies.
L1504: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1505: FDA agrees that the frequency and type of testing used in these studies was adequate to assess the safety of vimseltinib.
L1506: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1507: ## Deaths
L1508: ## Data:
L1509: No participants died in the double-blind period of MOTION.
L1510: In Pool 1, one participant in the open-label period of MOTION died from a fall that was assessed as unrelated to vimseltinib.
L1511: In addition, 8 participants with MST in DCC-3014-01-001 experienced TEAEs leading to death. All were assessed as unrelated to vimseltinib.
L1512: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1513: Vimseltinib was not considered as a cause of death for any reported event.
L1514: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1515: FDA reviewed all the death narratives and agrees that vimseltinib was not considered a cause of death for any event. No deaths are therefore reported in Section 6 of product labeling.
