L1535: Note 2: Drug-related AEs reported after 30 days following the last dose of vimseltinib were considered treatment emergent.
L1536: Note 3: If an SOC or PT was reported more than once for a participant, the participant was counted only once in the incidence for that SOC or PT.
L1537: Note 4: Pool 1 included all participants with TGCT in Phase 1/2 and MOTION who received at least 1 dose of vimseltinib at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly.
L1538: Note 5: Data cutoff for Phase 1/2 study is 27 Dec 2023 and for MOTION is 22 Feb 2024.
L1539: Source: ISS Table 12.3.1, ADAE.
L1540: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1541: Participants in the vimseltinib arm experienced more SAEs than those in the placebo arm in the double-blind period of MOTION; (b) (4)
L1542: Serious AEs including drug-related SAEs were generally consistent across the different pooled populations. No individual drug-related SAE was reported in more than participant.
L1543: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1544: FDA does not agree with Applicant's assessment, as all SAEs are considered attributable regardless of causality. Patients who received vimseltinib experienced more SAEs than those in the placebo arm (7% vs. 2.6%, respectively) with each SAE occurring in one patient only. FDA reviewed the narratives for all patients who experienced an SAE. Based on the reviewer assessment, only cellulitis (1 patient) and sub-cutaneous abscess (1 patient) were considered SAEs (2.4%). The original proposed labeling from the Applicant did not include SAEs but through negotiations, an incidence of 2.4% was included in product labeling.
L1545: ## **Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects**
L1546: ## Data:
L1547: In the double-blind period of MOTION, 5 (6.0%) participants in the vimseltinib arm discontinued vimseltinib treatment due to a TEAE compared to 0% in the placebo arm. The TEAEs were periorbital oedema, (b) (4) peripheral sensory neuropathy, rash, and hypertension. None of these TEAEs led to discontinuation in &gt;1 participant.
L1548: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1549: Treatment-emergent AEs leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in 9.3% (17/183) of participants in Pool 1. In Pool 1, the most frequently reported TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were rash and periorbital edema (each reported in 1.6% of participants [3/183]).
L1550: Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3 the most frequently reported TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were rash and periorbital edema (each reported in 1.2% of participants [3/251]).
L1551: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1552: More vimseltinib-treated participants discontinued study drug treatment than placebo-treated participants in the double-blind period of MOTION. However, the rate of discontinuation was still low in vimseltinib-treated participants.
L1553: Treatment-emergent AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were generally consistent across the different pooled populations. TEAEs leading to discontinuation were similar to those that were managed with interruptions and dose reductions.
L1554: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1555: FDA does not agree with applicant's assessment. A total of 4.8% of patients receiving vimseltinib discontinued treatment due to a TEAE, including one patient with each of the following: periorbital edema, peripheral sensory neuropathy, rash and hypertension. FDA does not consider (b) (4) to be an adverse reaction at least possibly attributable to the use of vimseltinib.
L1556: ## **Dose Interruptions, Delays, and/or Reductions Due to Adverse Effects**
L1557: ## Data:
L1558: In the double-blind period of MOTION, TEAEs leading to dose modification (including dose reduction or interruption) were experienced by 62.7% of participants in the vimseltinib arm versus 10.3% in the placebo arm. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification in the vimseltinib arm were periorbital edema (9.6%); COVID-19, pruritus, asthenia, and blood CPK increased (8.4% each); and rash maculo-papular (7.2%).
L1559: In Pool 1, treatment-emergent AEs leading to dose modification occurred in 74.3% of participants. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification were blood creatine phosphokinase (CPK) increased (16.9% [31/183]), periorbital edema (13.7% [25/183]), asthenia (12.0% [22/183]), pruritus (9.8% [18/183]), rash maculo-papular (9.3% [17/183]), and COVID-19 (8.2% [15/183]).
L1560: Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3 the most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification were blood CPK increased (14.3% [36/251]), periorbital edema (12.0% [30/251]), and asthenia (8.8% [22/251]).
L1561: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1562: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1563: TEAEs leading to dose modification were generally consistent across the different pooled populations and were similar to those seen in the double-blind period of MOTION. Although most participants experienced a TEAE leading to dose modification, the RDI was high (\ge 75\%), as discussed in Section 8.1.2
L1564: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1565: FDA analyzed the dose interruptions or reductions in patients on the double-blind portion of MOTION. Dose reductions due to an adverse reaction occurred in 39% of patients who received vimseltinib. Adverse reactions leading to dose reductions in \ge 2\% of patients were rash, periorbital edema, peripheral edema, fatigue, pruritus, face edema, increased CPK, neuropathy, and hypertension. Dose interruptions due to adverse reaction abnormality occurred in 40% of patients who received vimseltinib. Adverse reactions leading to interruptions in \ge 2\% of patients included rash, fatigue, peripheral edema, increased CPK, periorbital edema, face edema, pruritus, neuropathy, and hypertension. The Applicant's statement above that "TEAEs leading to dose modification (including dose reduction or interruption) were experienced by 62.7% of participants in the vimseltinib" includes overlap between some patients who experienced both dose reductions and dose interruptions.
L1566: Table 48 displays the agreed upon adverse reactions in product labeling for vimseltinib.

L1597: ## *Drug-related TEAEs*
L1598: In the double-blind period of MOTION, drug-related TEAEs with \ge 5\% higher incidence with vimseltinib than placebo were periorbital edema, fatigue, face edema, asthenia, pruritus, blood CPK increased, AST increased, rash, rash maculo-papular, peripheral edema, hypertension, and eyelid edema.
L1599: In Pool 1, the drug-related TEAEs reported in \ge 25\% of participants were periorbital edema (44.8% [82/183]), blood CPK increased (42.1% [77/183]), fatigue (28.4% [52/183]), asthenia (27.9% [51/183]), headache (27.3% [50/183]), AST increased (26.2% [48/183]), pruritus (26.2% [48/183]), and face edema (25.1% [46/183]).
L1600: Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3 the drug-related TEAEs reported in \ge 25\% of participants were periorbital edema (42.2% [106/251]), blood CPK increased (40.2% [101/251]), and fatigue (29.1% [73/251]).
L1601: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1602: ## *Grade 3/4 TEAEs*
L1603: In the double-blind period of MOTION, Grade 3/4 TEAEs were experienced by 37.3% in the vimseltinib arm and 10.3% in the placebo arm. Grade 3/4 TEAEs experienced by &gt;1 participant in the vimselinib arm included blood CPK increased, hypertension, periorbital edema, pruritus, and asthenia.
L1604: In Pool 1, 53.0% (97/183) of participants reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs. The most frequently reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were blood CPK increased (23.5% [43/183]), hypertension (8.7% [16/183]), and pruritus (2.7% [5/183]).
L1605: Similar to Pool 1, in Pool 3, 53.8% (135/251) of participants reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs. The most frequently reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were blood CPK increased (21.1% [53/251]), hypertension (7.2% [18/251]), and AST increased, lipase increased, and fatigue (2.4% [6/251] each)
L1606: ## *Adverse Drug Reactions*
L1607: All reported TEAEs from both MOTION and the Phase 1/2 study were evaluated to identify those that were reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib. Adverse drug reactions were identified based on event incidence, difference between the vimseltinib and placebo arms in MOTION, temporal relationship, seriousness, severity, consistency across studies, nonclinical findings, and plausible mechanism of action.
L1608: Placebo-controlled data from the double-blind period of MOTION (DCO date 22 August 2023) were selected as the best available data for the presentation of adverse drug reactions in the label. Participants were counted once for each adverse drug reaction term, and incidence rates were based on the number of participants who initially received placebo or vimseltinib.
L1609: Table 47 summarizes the adverse reactions observed in MOTION through Week 25. Events that were coded under different terms in the database but that represent the same phenomenon were grouped together as a single adverse drug reaction. Preferred terms included within each grouped term are captured in the footnotes.

L1622: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1623: Adverse drug reactions were identified based on comprehensive assessment of available data and are presented above.
L1624: TEAEs, including drug-related TEAEs and Grade 3/4 TEAEs were consistent across the different pooled populations. The TEAE profiles in the pooled populations were similar to those observed in the double-blind period of MOTION.
L1625: Review of all TEAEs occurring in Years 2 through 4 did not reveal an increased incidence of events typically occurring with longer latency. No new or worsening cardiovascular toxicities,
L1626: secondary malignancies, renal or hepatic toxicities, or other clinically significant AEs were observed with long-term vimseltinib exposure.
L1627: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1628: The reviewer and the Applicant used alternative methodology of grouping of TEAEs which led to difference in incidences of some composite terms. In addition, the reviewer does not agree with the reporting of drug-related TEAEs and has evaluated all TEAEs regardless of causality. This is reflected in the labeling for vimseltinib. Table 48 includes the adverse reaction with the agreed upon composite terms and incidence of adverse reactions that occurred in \ge 10\% of patients receiving vimseltinib with a difference between arms of &gt;5\%.
L1629: FDA initially proposed the use of the group term edema to include the preferred terms periorbital edema, face edema, eye edema, eyelid edema, generalized edema, edema peripheral, and localized edema. The Applicant did not agree and requested the use of the group terms periorbital edema (eye edema, eyelid edema, periorbital edema), facial edema (face edema) and peripheral edema (generalized edema, peripheral edema, localized edema). The Applicant stated that the use of these separate grouped terms best reflected clinical presentation and management. FDA agreed to the use of these group terms.
L1630: The agreed upon preferred terms for each group term were as follows:
L1631: - Periorbital edema: eye edema, eyelid edema, periorbital edema
- Dry eye: dry eye, xerophthalmia
- Fatigue: Fatigue and asthenia
- Peripheral edema: generalized edema, peripheral edema, localized edema
- Rash: urticarial dermatitis, eczema, rash, rash pruritic, rash maculo-papular, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome, dermatitis acneiform, eyelid rash, rash erythematous, rash pustular, dermatitis, rash macular
- Neuropathy: peripheral neuropathy, paresthesia, hypoesthesia, peripheral sensory neuropathy

L1635: ## Laboratory Findings
L1636: ## Data:
L1637: ## Chemistry
L1638: During the double-blind period of MOTION, notable differences between vimseltinib and placebo were observed for alanine aminotransferase (ALT), AST, Alkaline phosphatase (ALP), creatinine, and cholesterol. The majority of shifts in serum chemistry parameters from baseline to worst post-baseline CTCAE Grade were normal to Grade 1. No other clinically meaningful abnormalities in serum chemistry parameters were observed.
L1639: Across all pools, the most frequently reported shifts in chemistry laboratory parameters (worsening from baseline in ≥20% of participants) were ALT increased, AST increased, cholesterol high, creatinine increased, hypertriglyceridemia, and hypoglycemia. Most of these reported shifts were worsening to &lt;Grade 3.
L1640: In Pool 1, the parameters that showed any shift in ≥20% of participants were ALT increased (27.2% [50/184]), AST increased (92.4% [170/184]), cholesterol high (52.7% [97/184])
L1641: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1642: creatinine increased (42.9% [79/184]), hypertriglyceridemia (27.2% [50/184]), and hypoglycemia (22.8% [42/184]). Of these, the parameters that showed worsening to Grade 3/4 were AST increased (0.5% [1/184]), cholesterol high (1.1% [2/184]), and hypertriglyceridemia (1.1% [2/184]).
L1643: Although ALT or AST increases were frequently observed in participants who received vimseltinib, a small proportion of elevations were \ge 3\times upper limit of normal (ULN). None of these elevations were accompanied by \ge 1\timesULN of total bilirubin. No Hy's Law cases were identified.
L1644: Similarly, the cholestatic screening plot does not suggest any occurrence of cholestatic drug induced liver injury. Although an increase in ALP &gt;2\timesULN occurred in 2 participants, this increase was not accompanied by an increase in total bilirubin.
L1645: Results were similar in Pool 3.
L1646: ## *Hematology*
L1647: During the double-blind period of MOTION, notable differences between vimseltinib and placebo shifts in hematology parameters from baseline to worst post-baseline CTCAE Grade were reported for leukocytes (overall) and neutrophils. No other clinically meaningful shifts in hematology parameters from baseline to worst post-baseline CTCAE Grade were reported in the vimseltinib treatment period.
L1648: In Pool 1, the parameters that showed any worsening in \ge 25\% of participants were neutrophil count decreased (36.4% [67/184]) and white blood cell decreased (33.2% [61/184]). Of these, few participants showed worsening to Grade 3/4 (2.7% [5/184] of participants with neutrophil count decreased and 0.5% [1/184] of participants with white blood cell decreased).
L1649: All observed laboratory abnormalities from both MOTION and pooled data were evaluated to identify those that were reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib. Considering event incidence, difference between the vimseltinib and placebo arms in MOTION, temporal relationship, seriousness, severity, consistency across studies, nonclinical findings, and plausible mechanism of action, laboratory abnormalities observed through Week 25 in MOTION and considered reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib are summarized in Table 49.

L1660: In addition, increased CPK was identified as a laboratory abnormality reasonably associated with the use of vimseltinib. The frequency of increased CPK cannot be determined from MOTION because CPK was not assessed at baseline. Skeletal muscle injury or other organ damage was not observed in patients with elevated CPK. There were no treatment discontinuations due to increased CPK reported in MOTION or the Phase 1/2 study.
L1661: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1662: Serum enzyme elevations are frequently observed in patients treated with CSF1R inhibitors, which are known to reduce Kupffer cells in the liver as part of the mechanism of action. None of the changes met Hy's Law criteria and there was no evidence of cholestatic hepatotoxicity, skeletal muscle injury, or other organ damage.
L1663: Decreased neutrophils were observed with vimseltinib treatment during double-blind period of MOTION but were not associated with symptoms.
L1664: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1665: In general, the most clinically important laboratory abnormalities appear to be increases in liver enzymes, which occurred without associated elevations in bilirubin, as shown in lab shift table, Table 50. Overall, the worsening in baseline of AST or ALT elevation (92% and 24%, respectively) in patients treated with vimseltinib was higher than in patients treated with placebo (10% and 15%, respectively). No patients experienced a Grade 3 or higher elevation in AST or ALT in the vimseltinib arm. Refer to Section 8.2.5 for further discussion and overall conclusions regarding hepatotoxicity risk with vimselinib.

L1675: for Pool 3, which included all patients treated on vimseltinib at any dose. Although there were several cases of myalgia, there were no cases of rhabdomyolysis.

L1677: ## Vital Signs
L1678: ## Data:
L1679: In Pool 1, the mean (STD) baseline systolic and diastolic blood pressures for all participants were 123.9 (13.01) and 75.3 (9.38) mmHg, respectively. A total of 38.6% (71/184) of participants experienced no shift in post-baseline systolic blood pressure (BP) during treatment, and 10.9% (20/184) of participants experienced a maximum shift in systolic BP from baseline from &lt;140 to ≥160 mmHg. A total of 46.2% (85/184) of participants experienced no shift in post-baseline diastolic BP, and 6.5% (12/184) of participants experienced a maximum shift in diastolic BP from baseline from &lt;90 to ≥100 mmHg.
L1680: Results for Pool 3 were similar.
L1681: No other clinically meaningful changes from baseline in vital sign measurements were observed.
L1682: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1683: Hypertension was identified as an adverse drug reaction of vimseltinib. Hypertension can be managed in clinical practice and is not an important safety concern for vimseltinib. No other clinically meaningful changes from baseline in vital sign measurements were observed.
L1684: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1685: FDA agrees with Applicant's assessment. Median baseline systolic blood pressure for the 83 patients who received vimseltinib was 124.1 (range: 100, 180) and the mean change from baseline at week 25 was +5.9 (range: -16, +36). Median diastolic blood pressure for the 83 patients who received vimseltinib was 76 (range: 51, 96) and the mean change from baseline at week 25 was +5 (range: -17, +24).
L1686: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1687: ## Data:
L1688: During the double-blind period in MOTION, 1 participant in the vimseltinib arm had post-baseline QTcF &gt;450 ms, and 13 (15.9%) participants had QTcF increase from baseline &gt;30 ms.
L1689: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
L1690: ## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1691: None of the worst post-baseline ECG changes in the vimseltinib arm was interpreted as clinically significant.
L1692: In Pool 1, 15.4% (28/184) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline &gt;30 ms and 1.1% (2/184) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline &gt;60 ms.
L1693: In Pool 3, 14.9% (36/253) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline &gt;30 ms and 1.2% (3/253) of participants experienced a QTcF increase from baseline &gt;60 ms.
L1694: No participant experienced QTcF prolongation &gt;500 ms and only 3 participants experienced QTcF prolongation above the baseline &gt;60 ms.
L1695: No participant experienced QTcF prolongation on multiple occasions. No observation was considered clinically significant, and none led to dose modification or treatment discontinuation.
L1696: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1697: Vimseltinib administration was not associated with clinically significant postbaseline changes in ECG parameters.
L1698: Based on the integrated analysis, vimseltinib did not appear to have an effect on the QTcF interval.
L1699: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1700: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. One patient each in the placebo and vimseltinib arms experienced a post-baseline QTcF of &gt;450 ms. In the vimseltinib arm, 13 patients (16%) experienced a QTcF increase of &gt;30 ms. In the placebo arm, 7 patients (18%) experienced a QTcF increase of &gt;30 ms. These were not assessed as clinically relevant.
L1701: ## QT
L1702: ## Data:
L1703: A concentration QTc modeling analysis was performed based on the data from participants with MST in the Phase 1/2 study and healthy participants from Study DCC-3014-01-002 (who are physiologically similar to participants with TGCT). The results from the cardiodynamic evaluation of the 40 mg and 50 mg doses demonstrated that vimseltinib has no clinically relevant effect on heart rate or on cardiac conduction (the PR and QRS intervals). There was no clinically meaningful effect on the QTc interval, and an effect on \Delta\DeltaQTcF exceeding 10 ms can be excluded within the observed ranges of plasma concentrations of vimseltinib, up to 1774 ng/mL.
L1704: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1705: At exposures exceeding 2 times the mean maximum exposure of the recommended dosage, vimseltinib does not prolong the QTc interval to any clinically relevant extent.
L1706: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1707: FDA review by the QT-IRT team determined that at the maximum recommended dose of vimseltinib, clinically significant QTc interval prolongation was not observed.

L1713: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1714: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1715: Adverse drug reactions and laboratory abnormalities are discussed above. No additional submission-specific safety issues have been identified.
L1716: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1717: As previously mentioned, worsening in baseline of AST or ALT elevation (92% and 24%, respectively) in patients treated with vimseltinib was higher than in patients treated with placebo (10% and 15%, respectively) however no patients experienced a Grade 3 or higher elevation in AST or ALT in the vimseltinib arm and there were no bilirubin elevations, Hy's Law cases or other signs of severe liver toxicity in the submitted safety datasets for MOTION.
L1718: Nonetheless, the risk of hepatotoxicity or liver injury was identified as serious risk during the development of pexidartinib which shares the same target; CSF1R. Pexidartinib was the first CSF1R targeting inhibitor that was FDA-approved for systemic use in patients with TGCT. Pexidartinib was approved based on the clinical trial ENLIVEN, which treated 61 patients, and a total of 768 patients received pexidartinib during all clinical trials for which data was available at the time of pexidartinib approval.
L1719: To enroll on ENLIVEN, patients were required to have total bilirubin and AST/ALT \le 1.5 times the upper limit of normal (ULN). In ENLIVEN, serum transaminase elevations occurred in a majority of patients; elevations in ALT and AST occurred in 67% and 90% of patients, respectively; bilirubin increases occurred in 12% of patients. Grade 3 or higher elevations in ALT and AST occurred in 20% and 12% of patients, respectively. Grade 3 or higher bilirubin elevation occurred in 3.3%. Concerningly, approximately 5% of patients in the ENLIVEN trial experienced a pattern of serum transaminase and bilirubin elevation indicative of severe liver injury, characterized by AST or ALT greater than 3 times the ULN with concurrent bilirubin increases greater than 2 times the ULN.
L1720: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1721: Among the 768 patients in clinical trials, there were two cases of irreversible cholestatic liver injury; one patient with advanced cancer and ongoing liver toxicity died and one patient with a confirmed case of VBDS required a liver transplant. The review team thus instituted a boxed warning in product labeling for pexidartinib regarding serious and potentially fatal liver injury, and pexidartinib was made available only via the risk evaluation and mitigation (REMS) program with an Elements to Assure Safe Use (ETASU). The REMS program (n=609) revealed similar numbers observed of serious liver injury, with 32 patients (5.3%) developing liver toxicity within 71 days of the first dose of pexidartinib; ten required hospitalization, and two developing VBDS.
L1722: Because of the known risk of hepatotoxicity with pexidartinib as described above, FDA reviewed vimseltinib's NDA carefully for evidence of similar toxicity and evaluated the possible need for a REMS and/or a boxed warning in product labeling. The evaluation of hepatotoxicity for vimseltinib was primarily based on MOTION but also includes data collected from DCC-3014-01-001 and is described below.
L1723: ## Hepatotoxicity in MOTION
L1724: In the original protocol for MOTION, the risk of hepatotoxicity was mitigated with selection of patients without baseline liver toxicity, laboratory and clinical monitoring for liver toxicity, dosage interruptions and management guidelines for hepatobiliary laboratory abnormalities.
L1725: Patients treated on MOTION had AST, ALT and bilirubin assessed prior to treatment initiation, and then at day 1 and 15 of the first cycle, and the first day of each 28-day cycle thereafter. Patients treated on ENLIVEN, the pivotal study of pexidartinib, had a similar evaluation schedule, with ALT, AST and bilirubin assessed prior to initiation of treatment, weekly during cycle 1, and then once per 28-day cycle thereafter. It is unlikely that clinically significant LFT elevation events would have been missed in MOTION, as the monitoring schedule was fairly rigorous.
L1726: Table 51 lists all observed cases of laboratory abnormalities related to liver function in patients enrolled in MOTION. While AST and ALT elevations from baseline were common, almost all were Grade 1 in severity with only 3.6% experiencing Grade 2 events of AST increase, and no Grade 3 or higher events observed in any patients. There were no dose reductions or discontinuations due to liver function elevations and no SAEs related to hepatotoxicity.
L1727: **Table 51** FDA-Laboratory Values Related to Liver Function Worsening from Baseline in \ge 10\% of Patients Receiving Vimseltinib, MOTION

L1738: ## Hepatotoxicity in the overall clinical development program (Pool 3)
L1739: Additional analyses that include all 253 patients in Pool 3 are summarized below. This pool includes patients with solid tumors who may have had liver metastases and elevated liver chemistries at baseline. Overall, despite more liberal inclusion criteria in the broader database, very few Grade 3 events of AST elevation (2.4%) or ALT elevation (0.8%) were observed, with no Grade 3 bilirubin elevation and no Hy's Law cases.

L1743: In terms of timing and resolution of observed transaminase elevations, the median time to initial increase in AST/ALT was 2.1 weeks (range: 1.0-72.3) and 4.1 weeks (range 1.0, 151.6), respectively. Median time to initial increase in bilirubin was 16.1 (range 1.0-126.3) weeks. These data point to early onset of these mild transaminase increases, with median time to onset occurring within the first cycle of study drug. In Pool 3 (n=253), 26 patients had Grade \ge 2 AST or ALT elevations. Of these, 21 patients returned to Grade 1 or baseline. Of the 5 remaining patients that did not return to Grade 1 or baseline, the elevations did not worsen beyond Grade 2. The median time to resolution to Grade 1 or baseline for Grade \ge 2 ALT or AST elevation was 3.3 weeks.
L1744: In terms of reported management of these LFT elevations, among the 37 patients in Pool 3 with a reported AE of ALT increased, 2 patients experienced a dose modification (interruption and/or dose reduction) and none withdrew treatment due to ALT increased. Among the 66 patients in Pool 3 with a reported AE of AST increased, 9 experienced a dose modification and 1 withdrew treatment due to AST increased. There were only 2 patients with AEs of blood bilirubin increased, which did not lead to a dose modification or discontinuation. Among the 10 patients with dose interruptions due to an AE of ALT/AST increased, 9 were rechallenged, with 5 patients experiencing recurrent AEs of ALT/AST increased of the same or lower grade (positive rechallenge).
L1745: Review of the overall safety database was generally concordant with the data from patients treated in MOTION and did not raise concern that clinically significant hepatotoxicity had occurred across the larger development program, despite inclusion of some patients with liver metastases and/ or mildly elevated liver chemistries at baseline.
L1746: ## Overall Assessment
L1747: Given the relatively limited number of patients treated with vimseltinib and the known severe, irreversible liver toxicity observed with pexidartinib, FDA considered the need for a boxed warning or REMS program to ensure the safe use of vimseltinib. The primary review team discussed the safety findings and need for a REMS or boxed warning informally with FDA's Division of Risk Management (DRM), the Office of Oncologic Diseases (OOD) safety team, and the labeling team during the course of the evaluation of liver toxicity.
L1748: After extensive multidisciplinary discussions, review of nonclinical findings, presented safety data, and other literature, the review team determined that there is no current evidence of severe hepatotoxicity in patients treated with vimseltinib. Unlike in the pexidartinib development program, for patients treated with vimseltinib, there were no cases of Grade 3 or higher elevations in AST or ALT on MOTION, no cases of Hy's law or DILI, no patients required discontinuation of treatment due to elevated AST/ALT and no patients developed symptomatic liver toxicity. A total of 253 patients were treated with vimseltinib and there were no reported cases of liver injury. ENLIVEN, the registrational trial for pexidartinib, exhibited much more
L1749: concerning evidence for hepatotoxicity, for example with Grade 3 or higher elevations in ALT in 20% for patients and Hy's Law cases in 5% of patients. If vimseltinib was truly associated with concerning hepatotoxicity to the same extent, a similar percentage of patients would likely have exhibited evidence of hepatotoxicity in MOTION. In contrast, there were no Grade 3 elevations or HY's Law cases in MOTION, which is reassuring regarding the likely absence of severe risk for hepatotoxicity with vimseltinib.
L1750: In discussion regarding the use of a boxed warning, the review team did not find evidence to suggest that there was a risk of serious, fatal, or life-threatening consequences that would require alerting patients and providers in an enhanced manner. Regarding a discussion of the need for a REMS, given the low-grade events, the review team did not feel that patient selection, careful monitoring, avoiding, certain concomitant therapy, addition of another drug or managing patients in a manner, or avoiding use in a specific clinical situation was required for administration of vimseltinib. Although class labeling could be considered based on the known risk of pexidartinib, the safety signal for hepatotoxicity was much higher and more severe with the available clinical trial data on pexidartinib at the time of approval action compared with the data available at the time of approval for vimseltinib.
L1751: The Applicant did not originally propose to include any mention of hepatotoxicity in product labeling for vimseltinib. To further clarify the Applicant's stance, FDA sent an information request to the Applicant on October 24, 2025, requesting additional information to support the Applicant's position that hepatotoxicity with pexidartinib does not reflect a class effect and that no labelled warning was required for safe use of vimseltinib. The Applicant provided review of published literature that suggested that the molecular structure of pexidartinib undergoes extensive metabolism involving the formation of reactive intermediates with potential to modify biological macromolecules. The Applicant argued that formation of such reactive species has previously been associated with drug induced liver injury (DILI) and overt hepatotoxicity in humans across a range of chemotypes and pharmacological mechanisms unrelated to CSF1R inhibition. The mechanism of hepatotoxicity may be related to metabolism of the pyrrole ring of pexidartinib by CYP3A-mediated oxidation, which is not generalizable to all CSF1R inhibitors and non-clinical data via published literature suggested that pexidartinib-induced hepatotoxicity may be due to mitochondrial injury. The Applicant thus argued that this mechanism of hepatotoxicity was specific to pexidartinib and was not relevant to vimseltinib. While the FDA review team, including nonclinical pharmacology/ toxicology, reviewed the referenced literature and thought that the argument advanced by the Applicant might be plausible, the team concluded that the risk of hepatotoxicity related to agents targeting CSF1R could not be fully ruled out based on clinical and non-clinical data (See Section 5).
L1752: Given that there is some degree of residual uncertainty of the potential for hepatotoxicity based on the reviewed vimseltinib data given its relatively small safety database, enrollment of patients with no baseline hepatic liver abnormalities or disease on MOTION, observed low-grade LFT elevations in the majority of patients, and unclear definitive mechanism of hepatotoxicity with the CSF1R inhibitor pexidartinib, the review team determined that a Warnings and Precautions
L1753: for hepatotoxicity was warranted. Within the warning, the review team included a statement to avoid use of vimseltinib in patients with pre-existing increased serum transaminases, total bilirubin or direct bilirubin (&gt;ULN), or active liver or biliary tract disease, including ALP. This is consistent with inclusion criteria for MOTION. In addition, the review team added recommended monitoring for LFTs to product labeling, including AST, ALT, total bilirubin, direct bilirubin, ALP and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT), prior to initiation of vimseltinib, twice a month for the first two months and once every 3 months for the first year of therapy and as clinically indicated thereafter. This schedule was instituted accounting for the observation that toxicity generally occurs early in the administration of pexidartinib, with most (96%) of cases of pexidartinib-induced liver injury occurring in the first two months of administration, thus presumably any cases of liver injury occurring with vimseltinib would be accounted for with the recommended schedule. The review team also added a table of recommended dose modifications for vimseltinib in cases of elevated AST, ALT, bilirubin, INR, or ALP.
L1754: To further assess the potential risk for hepatotoxicity, a PMR (see section 13) was issued that will include a patient registry to provide additional information to further characterize the risk of hepatotoxicity in the indicated population outside of the clinical trial setting and to elucidate the potential long-term risk of liver injury associated with vimseltinib treatment. In addition, an enhanced pharmacovigilance program will be instituted to capture patients who may not be included in the PMR registry.

L1780: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1781: ## Data:
L1782: No clinical studies have been performed to assess use of vimseltinib during pregnancy or lactation.
L1783: In animals, malformations of the cardiovascular and skeletal systems along with additional indications of developmental perturbation, including structural anatomic variations and indications of developmental delay, occurred at 15 mg/kg/day (~20 times the exposure at the recommended human dose). There were no adverse findings in the pregnant animals and the maternal NOAEL (no adverse effect level) was 15 mg/kg/day, the highest dose evaluated. Due to the adverse malformations in the fetuses at 15 mg/kg/day, the developmental NOAEL was 5 mg/kg/day.
L1784: One pregnancy was reported in the Phase 1/2 study. One additional pregnancy was reported in a female partner of a male participant in the open-label period in MOTION. No evidence of birth
L1785: defects was noted in prenatal testing, and a healthy male newborn was delivered via cesarian section.
L1786: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1787: Pregnant women should be advised of the potential risk to the fetus.
L1788: Women should be advised not to breastfeed during treatment with vimseltinib.
L1789: Women of reproductive potential should be advised to use effective contraception during treatment with vimseltinib and for (b)/(4) days after the final dose.
L1790: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1791: FDA agrees that women of childbearing potential should be advised to use effective contraception during treatment and for at least (b)/(4) days after the final dose due to the potential risk to the fetus and that women should be advised not to breastfeed during treatment.
L1792: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1793: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1794: Vimseltinib has not been assessed in pediatric studies. See Section 10.
L1795: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1796: FDA agrees with this statement and there is no current plan to study vimseltinib in pediatric patients. The pediatric section of product labeling will contain a statement that the safety and effectiveness of vimseltinib have not been established in pediatric patients. Refer to non-clinical Section 5 for discussion regarding potential risks of vimseltinib in skeletally immature patients.
L1797: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1798: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1799: Based on the mechanism of action, drug abuse, withdrawal, or rebound effects are not anticipated with vimseltinib. There have been no cases of treatment overdose reported in any clinical study of vimseltinib. Treatment of overdose consists of observation and general supportive measures.
L1800: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1801: FDA agrees with this statement.
L1802: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1803: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1804: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1805: Not applicable. Vimseltinib has not been marketed in any country.
L1806: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1807: FDA agrees with this statement.
L1808: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1809: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1810: Very common and common toxicities appear to have been adequately represented in the studies with vimseltinib. Potential safety concerns with uncommon or rare occurrence may be detected during the postmarketing period. Routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for these adverse events.
L1811: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1812: FDA does not agree. Because uncertainty exists with respect to hepatotoxicity, a postmarketing requirement will be issued to assess the potential risk of severe hepatotoxicity. (See Section 13). An enhanced pharmacovigilance plan to collect data on events in the postmarketing setting for patients not captured in the registry was instituted.
L1813: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1814: ## Data:
L1815: A total of 253 participants constituted the vimseltinib safety database from the MOTION and Phase 1/2 studies, of whom 216 were participants with TGCT. The safety profile of participants with TGCT enrolled in the Phase 1/2 study were similar to those enrolled in MOTION.
L1816: In Pool 1 of the integrated analysis (184 participants), the mean (STD) duration of exposure to vimseltinib was 14.5 months (9.28).
L1817: Most participants (99.5%) reported at least 1 TEAE. The most frequently reported (\ge 25\% of participants) TEAEs in participants receiving vimseltinib were periorbital edema (45.4%), blood CPK increased (42.6%), and headache (33.9%). Of these, most were low grade in severity, nonserious, and considered drug-related.
L1818: The most frequently reported Grade 3/4 TEAEs were blood CPK increased (23.5%), hypertension (8.7%), and pruritus (2.7%).
L1819: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1820: Serious AEs were reported in 18.0% of participants. The most frequently reported SAEs were cellulitis (2.7%) and fall (1.1%). Most SAEs were considered unrelated to vimseltinib, and no related SAEs were experienced by &gt;1 participant.
L1821: Treatment-emergent AEs leading to dose modification (reduction or interruption) were reported in 74.3% of participants. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to dose modification were blood CPK increased (16.9%), periorbital edema (13.7%), asthenia (12.0%), pruritus (9.8%), rash maculo-papular (9.3%), and COVID19 (8.2%).
L1822: Treatment-emergent AEs leading to treatment discontinuation were reported in 9.3% of participants. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were rash and periorbital edema (each reported in 1.6% of participants).
L1823: Review of all TEAEs occurring in Years 2 through 4 did not reveal an increased incidence of events typically occurring with long latency. No new or worsening cardiovascular toxicities, secondary malignancies, renal or hepatic toxicities, or other clinically significant AEs were observed with long-term vimseltinib exposure.
L1824: No drug-related events leading to death occurred in the entire safety population.
L1825: Serum enzyme elevations including ALT, AST, ALP, and CPK were observed. None of the changes met Hy's Law criteria and there was no evidence of cholestatic hepatotoxicity, skeletal muscle injury, or other organ damage.
L1826: Decreased neutrophils were observed but were not associated with symptoms.
L1827: Overall, vimseltinib was generally safe with a manageable AE profile in participants with TGCT.
L1828: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1829: The evaluation of the safety data from participants with TGCT who received vimseltinib treatment at the recommended dose of 30 mg twice weekly in MOTION and Phase 1/2 demonstrated an acceptable safety profile.
L1830: Most of the TEAEs observed were nonserious, Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity, and manageable in clinical practice.
L1831: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1832: In general, FDA agrees with Applicant's assessment. FDA reviewed the following:
L1833: - 83 patients who received vimseltinib in MOTION at the proposed dose.
- 218 patients who were treated in clinical studies, including the 83 patients in MOTION and included 135 in DCC-2013-01-001, and
- 253 patients including the 218 above and 35 patients who received vimseltinib in the open-label period.
L1834: FDA specifically reviewed data regarding hepatotoxicity and noted no cases of Hy's law in this
L1835: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L1836: safety population. Refer to section 5.1 of product labeling for additional information. In review of the 253 patients who received vimseltinib (Pool 3) no additional safety findings, signals or serious adverse reaction were identified.
L1837: Patients treated on MOTION had AST, ALT and bilirubin assessed prior to treatment initiation, and then at day 1 and 15 of the first cycle, and the first day of each 28-day cycle thereafter. Patients treated on ENLIVEN, the pivotal study of pexidartinib for treatment of TGCT, had ALT, AST and bilirubin assessed prior to initiation of treatment, weekly during cycle 1, and then once per 28-day cycle thereafter.
L1838: Treatment with vimseltinib resulted in frequent AST and ALT elevations, without Hy's law or evidence of other liver dysfunction, and after extensive multidisciplinary review of the safety data, nonclinical findings and literature, it is FDA's opinion that there is no evidence to date of severe hepatotoxicity in patients treated with vimseltinib. However, post-marketing monitoring will be required, given the small sample size treated.
L1839: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1840: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1841: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1842: The MOTION trial demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in ORR, BPI-30 response rates, mean change from baseline to Week 25 in active ROM, and PROMIS-PF. The robustness of results was established based on extensive sensitivity analysis corresponding to each of these endpoints as presented in the "Study results" section of this review.
L1843: The results of the key secondary endpoint of ORR at Week 25 per TVS was statistically significant; however, this endpoint has not been validated as a surrogate for any of the established endpoints for clinical benefit claims in oncology, and therefore FDA considers ORR by TVS as supportive.
L1844: FDA concludes that MOTION demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in ROM for vimseltinib compared to placebo. The magnitude of improvement (difference in LS means) from baseline for patients on the vimseltinib arm compared to placebo arm was estimated to be 14.6% (4.0, 25.3). Although a treatment benefit was demonstrated for ROM, interpretation of the effect is unclear due to limited information on clinical meaningfulness for the target patient population in study MOTION. Based on the exploratory responder analysis using Applicant's proposed clinically meaningful threshold of 10%, there appears to be clinical benefit of vimseltinib on active ROM because more patients in the vimseltinib arm (48%) had a clinically meaningful improvement in ROM in the affected joints compared to the placebo arm (20%).
L1845: The results for 15-item PROMIS-PF in vimseltinib arm were statistically significant compared to placebo arm (Difference in LS means for change from baseline to Week 25: 3.3 [95% CI: 1.4, 5.2]). Due to limited information provided by the Applicant for evaluation of proposed clinical meaningful change, a definitive conclusion regarding the clinically meaningful difference cannot be made. However, based on FDA's assessment, a plausible clinical meaningful change may range from 3 to 7-points, and the observed effect of 3.3 points was covered by the range.
L1846: BPI-30 response rate was significantly different between the arms. Additional sensitivity analyses were performed to assess the robustness of the observed results and also beyond the Applicant specified definition. The sensitivity analyses support the primary analyses results and concludes that there is a substantial improvement(decrease) in the pain experienced by the patients treated with vimseltinib when compared to placebo.
L1847: Results from the Worst Stiffness NRS and EQ-VAS are not considered as part of improvement in quality-of-life assessments.
L1848: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1849: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1850: Based on the totality of evidence, the multidisciplinary review teams recommend granting regular approval of vimseltinib 30 mg BIW for the treatment of adult patients with symptomatic TGCT for which surgical resection will potentially cause worsening functional limitation or severe morbidity.
L1851: The Applicant submitted substantial evidence of safety and effectiveness from the pivotal MOTION Study to support approval. MOTION demonstrated that vimseltinib provided clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in ORR. The ORR was also statistically different between the two arms with 40% (95% CI: 29, 51) and 0% (95% CI: 0, 9) in the vimseltinib, and placebo arms, respectively. The responses were durable with a median duration that was NR in the vimseltinib arm with a range from 2.5+ to 19.4+ months. Of the 33 patients who treated with vimseltinib who experienced an objective response, duration of response was \ge 6 months for 85% of patients and \ge 9 months for 58%. These results were further supported by improvements in ROM and clinical outcome assessment data of patient-reported pain, and patient-reported physical functioning data.
L1852: In general, the safety findings were consistent with the known adverse reactions commonly seen with the use of other CSFRI inhibitors. The reviewer did not find evidence of severe or life-threatening hepatotoxicity with the use of vimseltinib at the proposed dose in an overall small sample size. Low-Grade elevation of AST and/or ALT were noted without a concurrent increase in bilirubin. There were no deaths, or cases of Hy's law or DILI. A Warnings and Precautions and dose modifications for hepatotoxicity were added to the USPI for vimseltinib. A PMR and enhanced pharmacovigilance plan for further assessment of hepatotoxicity were issued.

L1892: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1893: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1894: Pexidartinib was approved by the FDA for patients with TGCT associated with severe morbidity or functional limitations and not amenable to surgery. However, in the pivotal study to support approval, ENLIVEN, 3 of 61 (5%) patients who received pexidartinib developed signs of serious liver injury. Hepatotoxicity including liver failure and life-threatening vanishing bile duct syndrome (VBDS), ductopenia, and symptomatic cholestasis can occur and can occur despite monitoring and prompt drug cessation. Thus, pexidartinib has a boxed warning for hepatotoxicity and a REMS mitigate the risk of serious and potentially fatal liver injury. Given the important identified risk of severe hepatotoxicity observed with the use of pexidartinib, the review team performed a comprehensive analysis of the risk of hepatotoxicity for vimseltinib. The review team considered the need for a REMS based on a class effect and the identified risk for hepatotoxicity with use of pexidartinib. However, the safety data from the 253 patients who received at least one dose of vimseltinib in MOTION or the supportive study, DCC-3014-01-001, did not identify a risk of serious or significant hepatotoxicity that would require a REMS. While transient AST and ALT elevations were common in MOTION, there were no cases of bilirubin elevation and no cases of Hy's law.
L1895: The safe use of vimseltinib can be adequately implemented in the postmarketing setting without issuing a REMS for this drug product. The product labeling for vimseltinib includes information on common and clinically significant adverse reactions that have been observed across the drug class including hepatotoxicity, and trained oncologists have experience in the monitoring and management of these adverse reactions. Product labeling also includes dose modification and management guidelines any events as well as specific guidelines for hepatotoxicity. Risk management based on labeling, routine pharmacovigilance, and a PMR for hepatotoxicity is expected to ensure the safe use of vimseltinib.
L1896: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1897: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1898: The following postmarketing requirement with milestone goal dates were issued to the Applicant.
L1899: ## Clinical
L1900: **4791-2** Conduct a prospective clinical registry to evaluate the potential risk of serious hepatotoxicity in patients receiving vimseltinib for the treatment of tenosynovial giant cell tumor. Analyses should include evaluation of alanine aminotransferase, aspartate aminotransferase, bilirubin, symptoms suggestive of hepatotoxicity, and dose reduction and treatment discontinuation related to hepatotoxicity. Include investigations of time to event and severity, and potential risk factors such as age, race, and comorbidities. The registry should aim to enroll at least 100 patients. Monitor patients until discontinuation of study treatment or a minimum of 5 years from start of treatment, whichever occurs first. Interim safety analyses should be performed yearly beginning one year after the first patient has received vimseltinib to characterize the long-term risk of hepatotoxicity with vimseltinib.

L2011: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L2012: ## 19.4.1. Population PK Analysis
L2013: ## 19.4.1.1. Executive Summary
L2014: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2015: The PK of vimseltinib in adult population was characterized by a 2-compartment model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption followed by first-order elimination. The population PK model described the observed data reasonably well. The proposed dosage of 30 mg biw is considered acceptable for the proposed patient population.
L2016: ## 19.4.1.2. PPK Assessment Summary
L2017: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2018: [Highlight the key findings in the white cells.]
L2019: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	The aim of this analysis was to characterize the population PK of vimseltinib in healthy volunteers, patients with malignant solid tumors, and patients with TGCT.
Studies Included	DCC-3014-01-001, DCC-3014-01-002, and DCC-3014-03-001
Dose(s) Included	Treatment range: 4- 50 mg
Regimens: daily, 5 days daily and twice weekly after, 5 days daily and twice weekly after, twice weekly and single dose
L2020: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
L2021: ## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L2022: Population Included	98 Healthy volunteers (28%), 37 Malignant solid tumor patients (11 %), 214 Tenosynovial giant cell tumor patients (61%)
Population Characteristics (PopPK Report Table 1)	General	Age (year) [median (min, max)]: 45.0 (20.0, 91.0)
Weight (kg) [median (min, max)]: 76.0 (43.0, 150)
Sex: 204 Female (58%), 145 Male (42%)
Subject type (N [%]): 98 Healthy volunteers (28%), 37 Malignant solid tumor patients (11 %), 214 Tenosynovial giant cell tumor patients (61%)
Race (N [%]): 19 Black or African American (5%), 272 White (78%), 30 Unknown or not reported (9%), 28 Other (8%)
Organ Impairment	Hepatic impairment – Liver function based on National Cancer Institute classification was not identified as a covariate in the PopPK analysis.
Renal impairment – eGFR was not identified as a covariate and no relevant differences in steady state exposure are anticipated for mild and moderate renal function (eGFR \ge 37.7 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2).
Pediatrics (if any)	None
No. of Patients, PK Samples, and BLQ	349 adult participants; 6199 PK samples (excluding LLOQ (739 records), Dose of 0 (11 records) and duplicates (5 records))
Sampling Schedule	Rich Sampling	DCC-3014-01-001:
Predose and at 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10-12, 24 hours post-dose on Day -7, Day 1 (Cycle 1), Day 8 (Cycle 1) and Day 1 (Cycle 2)
DCC-3014-01-002:Arm la-c (Period 1), Ann 2 (Period 1 and 2): Predose (Day 1) and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72, 96, 120, 144, 168, 216, 264, 312, 480 hours post-dose.
Arm lb. (Period 2): Predose (Day 1), and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72 hours post-dose.
Arm ld: Day 1 Predose and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 hours post-dose. Day 2 Predose and at 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72, 84, 96, 120, 144, 168, 192, 216, 264, 312 hours post Day 2 dose.

Final Model	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Software and Version	NONMEM (Version 7.5)	Yes
Model Structure	2-compartment model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption and first-order elimination from the central compartment.	Yes
Model Parameter Estimates	See Table 55 below for model parameter estimates.	Yes
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	All model parameters were estimated with good precision (RSE \le 18.3\%). IIV was small to moderate for most of the parameters and relatively high for MAT. Shrinkage was low for most of the parameters (\le 30.7\%) and moderate for IIV on Vp/F. See Table 55 for details.	Yes
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	The number of post-first-dose below limit of quantification (BLQ) observations relative to the total number of Vimseltinib plasma observation records in the derived data file was low and no alternative modelling approaches for handling these observations were deemed necessary. These records were excluded from model development.	Yes
GOF, VPC	Model diagnostics for the final model indicate a satisfactory predictive performance for vimseltinib plasma concentrations.	Yes
L2025: ## 4NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}
## ROMVIMZA (vimseltinib)
L2026: See Figure 23 below for VPC.	
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Body weight and food status were the most influential covariates on the primary parameters in the final PK model. Body weight was included using allometric scaling with fixed exponents, thus steady state exposure increased with lower body weight. The covariate-parameter relation of food status on MAT suggests that fed subjects had an approximately 6-fold longer mean absorption time than fasted subjects; however, this had a limited effect on steady state exposure metrics. Several exploratory covariate-parameter relationships were identified during covariate analysis: Healthy volunteers had a lower Vc/F, and higher Vp/F, compared to TGCT patients. Subjects of Black or African American race had a higher Vp/F compared to non-Black or African American subjects. However, the effect of all structural and exploratory covariates on steady state exposure parameters were within the 80%-125% limits.	Yes
The significant covariates on PK are not expected to have a clinically meaningful impact.
Analysis Based on Simulation (optional)	Predicted exposures provided in Table 54 below.	Yes

L2050: ## 19.4.1.3. PPK Review Issues
L2051: The impact of hepatic function on PK was evaluated using popPK analysis to support labelling. There was no hepatic impairment other than mild by NCI ODWG classification in a total of 11 patients (7 with TGCT) from Ph1/2 study DCC-3014-01-001 and MOTION study. The mild hepatic impairment was not identified as a significant covariate based on 159 observations from these 11 patients. Despite small patient number, the number of observations and the coverage for time after dose is considered adequate to characterize the effect of mild hepatic impairment on PK.

L2061: ## 19.4.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L2062: ## 19.4.2.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L2063: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2064: Lack of positive E-R relationships for efficacy endpoints was observed across steady-state exposure with a planned fixed dosage of 30 mg biw (median relative dose intensity of 74%). Similar ORR was observed between patients with and without dosage reduction that suggested the efficacy in patients who needed dose modifications was not impacted as a result of dose reduction.

L2090: ## 19.4.2.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2091: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2092: Positive E-R relationships was observed across steady-state exposure for periorbital edema, rash, and AST/ALT elevation. The lower dosage (i.e., 20 mg biw) is predicted to marginally improve safety.

Model Evaluation	Model diagnostics for the final time to first event models indicated a satisfactory predictive performance of the observed data.	
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
Time to first event of periorbital edema: All covariates except tumor type were not found to be statistically significant. The probability of having a periorbital edema event was decreased for subjects with MST, compared to subjects with TGCT.
Time to first event of AST elevation: The effects of sex and tumor type were found to be statistically significant. The probability of having an AST elevation event was increased in males, compared to females, and was increased for subjects with MST, compared to subjects with TGCT.
No covariates were identified for time to first event of rash or ALT.
Yes.
Despite that male sex is associated with a higher risk of AST elevation, the incidence of AST elevation is marginally increased in male TGCT patients who received 30 mg biw at steady state (95% in males vs 91% in females), likely due to lower exposures with heavier weights in males.

Simulation for Specific Population	Following vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly dosing, the median predicted proportion of events at Week 25 were 75.1%, 49.3%, 93.8%, and 31.5%, for periorbital edema, rash, AST elevation, and ALT elevation, respectively (Figure 24, above). The predicted proportions were similar across WT subgroups.	Yes.
Visualization of E-R relationships	The predicted proportions of safety events increased with increasing Cmax,ss, with an initial increase for low Cmax,ss, followed by a plateau. The initial increase was steep for AST elevation events, while it was less the case for other endpoints, leading to a relatively flat relationship between Cmax,ss and periorbital edema, rash and ALT elevation events (Figure 27).	
Yes.
CPK elevation (see 19.4.2.5) was evaluated whose incidence was not found to be increased with increasing exposures.

Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	Following vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly dosing, the median predicted proportion of events at Week 25 were 75.1%, 49.3%, 93.8%, and 31.5%, for periorbital edema, rash, AST elevation, d ALT elevation, respectively (Figure 28). Following vimseltinib 20 mg twice weekly dosing, the simulated difference in the median predicted proportions of events compared to 30 mg twice weekly dosing was <5% for periorbital edema, rash, and ALT elevation. For AST elevation, the difference was 7.3%.	
Yes.
AST elevation shows a steep E-R relationship which led to dose reduction in 2.5% of patients. Dose modification is considered an appropriate measure to manage this AE.

L2128: ## **19.4.2.5. ER Review Issues**
L2129: ## E-R relationship for CPK elevation
L2130: For CPK elevation that's drug-related, the incidence is 24% of any Grade and 10% of Grade\ge3 that led to dose reduction in 8.5% of TGCT patients in MOTION study. The E-R analysis was therefore requested by the reviewer and the results were submitted as a response to Information Request.
L2131: In Ph1/2 study DCC-3014-01-001 where CPK lab measurement was collected, similar elevation of CPK over time was observed in 3 cohorts of TGCT patients (cohort 5: 30 mg biw, cohort 8: 10 mg qd, cohort 9: 6 mg qd) (Figure 29). Notably, Cohort 5 and cohort 8 have similar exposures despite different dosing intervals. E-R analysis for maximal CPK change from baseline was evaluated using generalized linear model and non-linear models, and a continuous model with linear effect of Cavg was selected as the base model. The effect of Cavg,ss on maximal CPK change from baseline was found statistically insignificant (p-value = 0.719) and associated with high uncertainty, suggesting a lack of E-R relationship (Table 64). In the covariate analysis, tumor type was identified as a significant covariate where patients with TGCT were more likely to have greater CPK elevation compared to patients with MST.
L2132: Pooled E-R analysis using data from both Ph1/2 and MOTION studies was conducted to further evaluate if any Grade or Grade \ge3 CPK first elevation by CTCAE is dependent on exposure using Cox proportional hazard model. While there was a significantly positive effect of Cavg,ss for any Grade CPK first elevation (Table 65), the positive effect of Cavg,ss for Grade \ge3 CPK first elevation was marginally non-significant (p=0.0758) (Table 66). For 20 mg biw dosage, the predicted proportion of events at week 25 was only slightly lower than 30 mg biw dosage.
L2133: Compared to the 10 mg QD dose, the 30 mg BIW dose resulted in a 14% lower Cavg,ss and a 7% higher Cmax,ss. The predicted proportion of safety events was similar between the 10 mg QD and 30 mg BIW doses. However, it remains unclear whether a sustained higher Cavg/Cmin of 10 mg QD could lead to additional chronic safety concerns for QD dosing regimen.

L2145: ## 19.4.2.6. Reviewer's Independent Analysis
L2146: ## Efficacy with a high rate of dose reduction
L2147: Given AE leading to dose reduction occurred in 39% of patients early, relative to the median time to response, the impact on efficacy was evaluated. ORR by RECIST/TVS between patients with and without dose reduction at any time during treatment was compared, which showed similar % of responders (Table 67). Notably, the steady-state exposures in patients with dose reduction were higher than those without dose reduction, indicating dose modifications are needed to mitigate exposure driven toxicities. Considering these toxicities are generally reversible, the proposed dosage is considered acceptable.

L2151: ## 19.4.2.7. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L2152: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2153: An exposure efficacy analysis based on data from participants with TGCT who were randomized to receive 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen in MOTION did not show association between vimseltinib exposure and ORR per RECIST v1.1 or TVS. A flat exposure-efficacy relationship was identified across the exposure range for the evaluated dosing regimen. The exposure-efficacy model could not predict ORR per RECIST v1.1 or TVS outside of the 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen, as a substantial part of the simulated exposure distribution would be outside of the evaluated exposure range.
L2154: An exposure safety analysis including data from participants with TGCT who were randomized to receive 30 mg twice weekly dosing regimen in MOTION showed a positive and mostly shallow relationship between vimseltinib exposure and safety (periorbital edema, rash, ALT, and AST). Predictions from exposure-safety models showed that lower vimseltinib exposure may decrease the probability of safety events in some participants. For periorbital edema, rash, and ALT elevation, the difference in the median predicted proportions of these safety events at Week 25 was marginal at &lt;5% following 20 mg twice weekly, compared to 30 mg twice weekly. For AST
L2155: elevation, the difference was 7.3%. However, the predictions are associated with large statistical uncertainty.
L2156: Overall, the exposure response analysis supports vimseltinib 30 mg twice weekly as the optimal dosing regimen in patients with TGCT.
L2157: The FDA's Assessment:
FDA generally agrees that Applicant's position.
L2158: ## 19.4.3. PBPK Analysis
L2159: ## 19.4.3.1. Executive Summary
L2160: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate the drug-drug interaction (DDI) potentials of vimseltinib:
L2161: - as an inducer of CYP2B6
- as an inhibitor of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1/3 and OCT2
L2162: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK report ( [REDACTED] (b) (4) -report, seq 002), the response to FDA's information requests submitted on September 10th (seq 0010, clin-response-ir13-20240910), and the modeling supporting files to conclude the following:
L2163: - Vimseltinib was predicted to increase dabigatran AUC and C_{max} up to 3.0-fold when simultaneously administered and considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC50 value of P-gp. The inhibitory effects of vimseltinib may be mitigated by administering dabigatran 4 hours after vimseltinib dosing. (b) (4)
- Vimseltinib was predicted to increase rosuvastatin AUC and C_{max} up to 2.6- and 6.3-fold, respectively when simultaneously administered and considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC50 value of BCRP. (b) (4)
- The PBPK analysis of CYP2B6 interaction was not evaluated because (b) (4)
- The PBPK analysis of (b) (4)
L2164: ## 19.4.3.2. Background
L2165: Vimseltinib, also known as DCC-3014 and DP-6865, is a kinase inhibitor of the colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor (CSF1R) kinase currently being developed for treatment of adult patients with symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT). The available dose strengths are 14, 20 and 30 mg capsules. The recommended dosing regimen is 30 mg administered orally twice weekly at least three days apart. The tablets should be swallowed as a whole and can be taken with or without food.
L2166: Following oral administration of single doses of vimseltinib 6 to 50 mg in healthy subjects, vimseltinib C_{max} and AUC_{0-24h} were increased dose proportionally (DCC-3014-01-002). In patients with TGCT (Cohort 5 of Study DCC-3014-01-001), exposure on Cycle 2 Day 1 following twice weekly 30 mg doses was approximately 3.6-fold higher than exposure following a single 30 mg dose (Cycle 1 Day 1) (). Steady state was reached following approximately 5 weeks of 30 mg vimseltinib twice weekly administration (DCC-3014-01-001). Following administration of a single 30 mg vimseltinib with a standardized FDA high-fat meal in healthy subjects, the C_{max} of vimseltinib decreased approximately 30%, compared to the fasted state, and T_{max} was delayed by 5 hours. Its AUC_{0-inf} was comparable under the fasted and fed conditions (DCC-3014-01-002).
L2167: Vimseltinib has high permeability and pH-dependent solubility. It is well-absorbed and is extensively metabolized primarily via oxidation, N-demethylation, and N-dealkylation (DCC-3014-03-0023). The enzymes involved in the metabolism of vimseltinib were not identified when parent disappearance was monitored for the reaction phenotyping studies conducted using human liver microsomes and recombinant CYP enzymes due to low turnover of vimseltinib (DCC-3014-03-0007). In the human ADME study, approximately 37.5% and 42.9% of radioactivity were recovered in the urine (5.1% unchanged parent) and the feces (9.1% unchanged parent), respectively (DCC-3014-01-003).
L2168: Vimseltinib is not a competitive or time-dependent inhibitor of any of the commonly studied CYP enzymes (DCC-3014-03-0001). Vimseltinib is not an inducer of CYP1A2 and CYP3A4. Although dose-dependent induction of CYP2B6 was observed, the fold induction of CYP2B6 mRNA and activity was below 2-fold at the vimseltinib concentration that was 50 times of its unbound steady-state C_{max} (DCC-3014-03-0009). Vimseltinib is a substrate of P-gp (DCC-3014-03-0006). Vimseltinib inhibits P-gp (IC_{50}= 4.35 \muM), BCRP (IC_{50}= 0.556 \muM), OATP1B3 (IC_{50}= 10.1 \muM), OATP1B1 (IC_{50}= 9.21 \muM), MATE1 (IC_{50}= 7.58 \muM), MATE2K (IC_{50}= 23.3 \muM), OAT1 (IC_{50}= 51.5 \muM), OAT2 (IC_{50}= 23.7 \muM), and OCT2 (IC_{50}= 0.456 \muM) (DCC-3014-03-0006, DCC-3014-03-0024). The applicant conducted clinical DDI studies with itraconazole, and rabeprazole to evaluate some of the *in vitro* findings. Refer to the Clinical Pharmacology review section for detail information on vimseltinib regarding ADME properties, *in vitro* and clinical studies used in PBPK modeling.
L2169: ## 19.4.3.3. Methods
L2170: Simulations related to evaluation of vimseltinib as an inhibitor of transporters were performed using the PK/PD Profiles mode in the Simcyp® Simulator (Version 22 Certara, Sheffield, UK). Schemes of the PBPK simulation strategy are shown in Figure 30, which summarizes the studies used for vimseltinib model development and verification, and model applications in DDI predictions. The final model input parameters were summarized in Table 68. The vimseltinib PBPK model consists of an ADAM absorption model, a full PBPK model for distribution and an enzyme kinetics model for elimination. The Simcyp library files itraconazole Fasted soln, SV-bupropion, SV-dabigatran, SV-rosuvastatin, and were used for DDI simulations without any modification.

L2177: ## 19.4.3.4. Results
L2178: ## 1. Can the PBPK model adequately describe the PK profiles of vimseltinib?
L2179: Yes. The vimseltinib PBPK model could reasonably well describe vimseltinib PK following administration of single and multiple doses of vimseltinib in healthy subjects (Study DCC-3014-01-002) (data not shown), and in patients with TGCT (Study DCC-3014-01-001). The latter was simulated at the reviewer's request and the results are shown in Figure 31 and Table 69.

L2181: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219304}

L2188: ## 2. Can PBPK analyses be used to estimate the effects of vimseltinib on substrates of CYP2B6 and transporters?
L2189: ## Induction effects of vimseltinib on the CYP2B6 substrate bupropion
L2190: At the vimseltinib concentration that was 50 times of its unbound steady-state Cmax at the currently recommended dosing regimen, (b) (4) Therefore, vimseltinib is not considered as a clinical CYP2B6 inducer based on the ICH M12 Guideline on Drug Interaction Studies. Therefore, the PBPK analysis of induction effects of vimseltinib on the CYP2B6 substrate bupropion was not evaluated.
L2191: ## Effects of vimseltinib on the OATP1B and BCRP substrate rosuvastatin
L2192: The predicted effects of vimseltinib on rosuvastatin were inadequate for reasons detailed below.
L2193: (b) (4)
L2194: ## Effects of vimseltinib on the P-gp substrate dabigatran etexilate
L2195: Dabigatran etexilate is a P-gp substrate. Dabigatran is a metabolite of dabigatran etexilate and is not a P-gp substrate. The effects of P-gp inhibitors on dabigatran etexilate are assessed by changes in dabigatran plasma exposure. The ability of the PBPK models of dabigatran etexilate and dabigatran to predict P-gp interaction was assessed using clinical DDIs of dabigatran with verapamil, clarithromycin, quinidine, and ketoconazole (Dabigatran Simcyp summary report). Of note, the ketoconazole DDI data was also used in the model development. Large range of IC50 values of these inhibitors are reported in the Certara DDI Database. The difference in IC50 between minimum and maximum values of each inhibitor ranges from 22- to 7450-fold if all the results were considered, and 1.5- to 280-fold if only experiments conducted in the condition similar to that of vimseltinib (e.g. Caco-2 cells and (b) (4) as a probe substrate) were considered. In addition, the reviewer (b) (4)
L2196: The applicant was requested to simulate the effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure when the P-gp K_i was 50-, 100-, and 200-fold lower than the in vitro IC50, and the dose staggering scheme under the above conditions. The results are shown in Figure 33. When vimseltinib and dabigatran etexilate were administered simultaneously and considering a P-gp K_i value 200-fold lower than the in vitro IC50 value, vimseltinib was predicted to increase dabigatran AUC and C_{max} by approximately 2.6- and 3.0-fold, respectively, which were approximately 2-fold higher than that predicted using the K_i value calculated from the in vitro IC50 value using the Cheng-Prusoff equation (PMID: 4202581). In addition, the reviewer also simulated the effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure by reducing the P-gp IC50 value by 1000-fold. Vimseltinib was predicted to increase both dabigatran AUC and C_{max} by approximately 3.0-fold. When dabigatran etexilate was administered 4 hours after vimseltinib dosing, the inhibitory effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure were diminished (e.g. increases in AUC and C_{max} were approximately 1.25-fold) regardless of the K_i value used in the simulations. Therefore, despite uncertainties in the predicted interaction effect of vimseltinib, the potential risk for a clinically significant interaction between vimseltinib and the P-gp substrate dabigatran etexilate may be mitigated via dose staggering.
L2197: Of note, the Applicant (b) (4)
L2198: **Figure 33** Effects of vimseltinib on dabigatran exposure following staggered dosing
L2199: Ten virtual trials of 10 patients with cancer (50% female) aged 20 to 50 years receiving 150 mg dabigatran etexilate (P-gp probe substrate) as a single dose with and without co-administration of vimseltinib (30 mg given every 84 hours for a total of 46 days) were generated. All simulations were performed under steady-state conditions for vimseltinib. Staggering simulations were performed in which the dose of dabigatran etexilate was administered 0 to 4 hours after the last dose of vimseltinib (30 mg every 84 hours for 46 days). K_i values that were 50-, 100-, 200- and 1000-fold lower than in vitro IC_{50} value for P-gp were applied in these simulations. The simulated geometric mean AUC_{inf} and C_{max} values and corresponding GMRs for dabigatran with and without coadministration of vimseltinib are presented.
L2200: Source: Table 10 in clin-response-ir13-20240910

L2205: ## 19.4.3.5. Conclusions
L2206: - PBPK analysis was considered adequate to inform the interaction potential between vimseltinib and the P-gp substrate dabigatran. Vimseltinib was predicted to increase dabigatran AUC and C_{max} 2- to 3.0-fold when administered simultaneously and considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC50 of P-gp. However, dabigatran AUC and C_{max} were predicted to increase up to 1.3-fold when dabigatran etexilate was administered 4 hours after vimseltinib dosing regardless of the K_i value used in the simulations. Therefore, despite uncertainties in the predicted effect, the potential risk for a clinically significant interaction between vimseltinib and the P-gp substrate dabigatran etexilate may be mitigated via dose staggering. (b) (4)
- Vimseltinib was predicted to increase rosuvastatin AUC and C_{max} up to 2.6- and 6.3-fold, respectively when simultaneously administered and considering the uncertainty on the in vitro generated IC50 of BCRP. (b) (4)
- The PBPK analysis of CYP2B6 interaction was not evaluated because (b) (4)
- The PBPK analysis of vimseltinib interaction with (b) (4) is considered inadequate because the (b) (4)
